   produced, say so and STOP. BRAIN treats a worker's contradiction as a signal that
   BRAIN is wrong.

   eye reads as flashing. State the area affected and the rate before shipping any
   animated value.

4. RUN THE THING. Source-derived diagnoses have been wrong repeatedly here.

5. AUTHORITY. No commit to main, push, merge, release, install, launch or restart
   without an explicit creator yes.

6. CREATOR DATA. state.json is creator data and is gitignored. Never edit it.

7. MUTATION DISCIPLINE. Files are CRLF; string replacement silently fails. Use line
   indices and assert the mutation landed on disk.

8. REUSE OVER REINVENTION. The creator's standing objection: "I'd expect it to reuse
   the icons, but it didn't, which brings my fear that you needlessly make everything
   convoluted." If you are writing a renderer, layout, or interaction the workspace
   already owns, STOP and call the existing one. A copy is the same defect as a rewrite.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 003 (ACTIVE)
TRAIL AS ORDINARY FOLDER CONTENTS — START FROM SCRATCH ON main
======================================================================

WHY THIS IS A RESTART
Three attempts were rejected. All three failed the same way: they built a parallel
system beside the workspace instead of using it. BRAIN caused this by specifying
EFFECTS ("its own surface", "white on dark") instead of MECHANISMS. The creator's
instruction is now explicit: "pick the simplest path of adding the trails directly
inside the folder, without rewriting reinventing anything."

THE WHOLE FEATURE, IN ONE SENTENCE
When the creator is inside a folder, that folder's ANCESTORS appear in its item list as
ordinary items, outlined in --text, and cannot be deleted.

THE MECHANISM — BRAIN READ THE CODE THIS TIME
collectVisible(state, parentId, expandedSet) in public/graph-model-20260730b.js is the
single function deciding what is in a folder. It returns a flat list of
{id, parentId, kind, depth, siblingIndex, siblingCount}. EVERYTHING downstream — icons,
physics, edges, drag, hotkeys, selection, sets, expansion — consumes that list and does
not care how an entry got there.

So: PREPEND THE ANCESTORS TO THAT LIST. That is the feature. The ancestor walk already
exists (pathToRoot / pathToBin in workspace-20260730b.js). One function, one place.

WHAT YOU MUST NOT BUILD
No trail-graph.js. No trail-graph-model.js. No separate simulation. No overlay. No
expander button. No trail-specific renderer, markup, layout, drag path, or hit-testing.
No parallel hotkey path. If you find yourself creating a file with "trail" in its name,
STOP — the design has drifted back into what the creator rejected three times.

COLOUR — THE CREATOR CORRECTED BRAIN, AND BRAIN HAD IT WRONG
The creator's screenshot shows every item wearing a coloured OUTLINE around its icon
(orange, green, yellow), tile transparent. That ring is what they mean by colour.
It is drawn by public/styles/items.css:44-47 —
    .icon-item.folder-colored .item-icon {
      border: 3px solid var(--folder-color);
      background: color-mix(in oklch, var(--folder-color) 18%, var(--surface));
    }
So an ancestor's colour is exactly: --folder-color: var(--text). Nothing else.
White ring on dark, near-black ring on light, because --text already resolves per theme.

DO NOT set background: var(--text). Assignment 002 did, and it filled the folder shape
into a solid featureless block — the defect the creator hit. The fill stays as the
existing color-mix produces it. Do not touch .folder-art's border, background or
box-shadow. Do not add img filters. Do not add a new CSS block for this: set the custom
property and let the existing rule draw.

UNDELETABLE — REUSE THE EXISTING PRECEDENT
Assignment 002 needed guards in nine separate deletion paths because its trail nodes were
a foreign body type. Do not repeat that. This codebase ALREADY has a non-deletable
pseudo-entry in this same list: kind 'bin-origin' (see collectVisibleBin's originParentId
and the bin-origin-ghost rules in graph.css). Study how it stays out of selection and
deletion, and follow that pattern. If it turns out that pattern does not cover a path,
report which and ask — do not scatter new guards.

EXPANSION — REUSE THE EXISTING AFFORDANCE
collectVisible already recurses on expanded.has(candidate.id). An ancestor in the list
expands with the SAME affordance as any other folder. Do not add a gesture, a button, or
a shift/right-click path. Whatever expands a folder today expands an ancestor.
(The creator tried shift-right-click and found nothing: trail/02's expander was a
bespoke button BRAIN never specified. That whole approach is gone.)

CURRENT FOLDER EXCLUDED
The folder the creator is inside does NOT appear. The trail is the path TO here. At root,
report what the list shows (likely nothing).

OTHER BEHAVIOUR
Ancestors are ordinary items in every other respect: real icons via the existing icon
path, normal physics, normal drag with NO pinning and NO position persistence (nothing
about them is written to state.json — they are derived from the path), normal hotkeys
except deletion, click navigates to that folder.

SAFETY — STANDING RULE 2
These bodies join the creator's real simulation. Entering a folder must not visibly throw
their arranged items around. State what moves, how far, and for how long.

BOUNDARIES
No hue-assignment changes for real items. No new colour literals. No solver changes. Both
themes and transparent must hold. Do not commit to main, do not push, no release,
install, launch or restart. Do not touch state.json.

EVIDENCE
The creator wants to CORRECT A RUNNING BUILD rather than review captures — they said so
explicitly. So: get it working on main against their real state, keep the diff SMALL, and
report what you changed in plain terms. Capture depth 1, mid-depth and one expanded, both
themes, but do not gold-plate before they have looked. Expect correction and keep the
change easy to adjust.

Append WORKER PICKUP, then execute, then WORKER CHECKPOINT and stop.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] WORKER - Assignment 003 PICKUP
======================================================================

UNDERSTOOD
Start from scratch on main @ 5b6d95a. The feature is: prepend the current
folder's ancestors to the item list in collectVisible (one function, one
place). No trail-named files, no parallel system, no expander button, no
new CSS block, no hue changes, nothing written to state.json for the
ancestors. Colour is exactly --folder-color: var(--text) so the existing
items.css rule draws the ring and its color-mix fill.

DECISIONS I AM MAKING, ALL REPORTED IN THE CHECKPOINT
1. NEVER SELECT — the bin-origin pattern is "a marker checked where it
   matters, so it stays out of selection and therefore out of deletion".
   Ancestors carry an `ancestor-item` class; click navigates, right-click
   is inert, marquee and select-all/shift-ranges (visibleItemIds) filter
   them. Deletion then needs guards in exactly TWO command paths — the
   drag-drop commands, because ancestors are the one non-selectable body
   that is still draggable (ghosts are pointer-events:none). One injected
   isAncestorItem predicate, two guard sites, no scatter.
2. THE DRAG — a single-node drag branch in pointer-controller (an
   ordinary-body drag for a never-selectable body). Moves under the
   pointer, floats on release. NO pin (shift is ignored), NO persistence:
   release skips pinDraggedNodes/releaseDraggedNodes, because
   releaseDraggedNodes would delete a real folder's stored position where
   the ancestor id collides with one.
3. THE HEADS — the root ("As you Go") and Bin head DO render as ancestor
   tiles (pathTo already synthesises them). buildCandidate gets a one-line
   fallback for the two pseudo ids using the path's own name — no new
   kind. graphEdges loses its ROOT_ID/'bin' parent skip (the ids check
   already covers everything; a head that is not visible has no node).
4. EXPANSION — the existing [data-expand] chevron on the ancestor tile;
   expansion persists as the real folder's OWN graphExpandedGroupIds
   state (it is a real folder), which I will note in the checkpoint.
   Expanding the Bin head spawns the Bin top-level list (minus the
   current drilled folder).
5. CURRENT FOLDER EXCLUDED — collectVisible skips children whose id is
   the current parentId (a folder cannot be its own child, so the check
   is inert for the normal walk). At root the ancestor list is empty.
6. HUES — ancestors are excluded from the spatial hue graph (folderNodes
   filter + edge stroke loop), so real items' hue assignment is
   untouched; the ancestor ring comes only from --folder-color:
   var(--text) via the existing rule. No background override, no folder-
   art change, no img filters.
7. The empty-folder message hides when ancestors are present.

KNOWN GAP TO REPORT: on main, bin-origin ghosts are NOT filtered by the
marquee (they can be marquee-selected today) — the pattern as shipped has
that hole. I close it for ancestors; I will report the ghost hole and
ask, rather than touch ghosts without instruction.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] WORKER - Assignment 003 CHECKPOINT
======================================================================

IN PLAIN TERMS
The current folder's ancestors now appear in its item list as ordinary
folders, outlined with the theme text colour (white ring on dark mode,
near-black ring on light), current folder excluded. Click one and you
navigate into it. That is the whole feature.

THE ONE PLACE
public/graph-model-20260730b.js, collectVisible (and collectVisibleBin
for the Bin): the caller (the entry's updateGraphView) passes
pathTo(...).slice(0, -1) — the path to here, minus the current folder —
and the function prepends those entries to the folder's item list,
flagged ancestor:true. Everything downstream (icons, physics, edges,
drag, expansion, hotkeys, selection) treats them as ordinary bodies,
because they are.

WHAT CHANGED, PER FILE
- graph-model-20260730b.js: collectVisible/collectVisibleBin prepend the
  ancestors (root-to-leaf, depth = chain position, parented along the
  chain), skip the current folder when an expanded ancestor's children
  are walked, and walk expanded ancestors in place (the Bin head's
  children are the top-level Bin list). graphEdges: removed the old
  ROOT_ID/'bin' parent skip — an edge now draws exactly when both
  endpoints are visible, so chain edges draw and an absent head still
  gets none.
- workspace-20260730b.js: buildCandidate gains a one-line fallback for
  the two pseudo heads (root "As you Go", Bin) using the path's own
  names, and copies the ancestor flag. applyFolderColor sets
  --folder-color: var(--text) for ancestors and nothing else — the
  EXISTING items.css rule draws the 3px ring plus its color-mix fill.
  syncFolderColors excludes ancestors from the spatial hue graph and the
  edge recolouring, so real items' hue assignment is untouched. Tiles
  carry an `ancestor-item` class; click navigates (the exact same
  setNavigation/clearSelection/destroyGraphView/render/save sequence the
  pill breadcrumb buttons use); right-click is inert; visibleItemIds and
  the marquee filter them out of selection. The empty-folder message
  hides while ancestors are present.
- pointer-controller.js: one branch — an ancestor drags as a single
  body, moves under the pointer, floats on release. No pin (Shift is
  ignored), no persistence: pinDraggedNodes/releaseDraggedNodes are not
  called (releaseDraggedNodes would remove the real folder's stored
  position where the ancestor id collides with one).
- workspace-commands.js: dragDropToBin and dragDropToFolder refuse
  ancestor ids with a status line (one injected isAncestorItem
  predicate, two guard sites — the bin-origin pattern means ancestors
  never enter the selection, so no other path can reach them).
- marquee-controller.js: ancestor tiles excluded from marquee selection.
- Tests: model (prepend, exclusion, expansion, Bin variant, edges, and
  the safety settle measurement), pointer (ancestor drag), commands (the
  two guards), marquee (exclusion).

COLOUR — WHAT THE CAPTURES MEASURE
folderColor property = var(--text) on every ancestor tile; computed ring
= rgb(242,237,226) dark / rgb(41,40,34) light (= --text); fill =
color-mix(18% --text over surface) — the existing rule's own fill, NOT a
solid block. No background override, no folder-art change, no img
filters, no new CSS block, no colour literals.

CLICK NAVIGATES — PROBED
Clicked the deepest ancestor tile (Real) through the real click path in
a live run: breadcrumb went "As you Go > Real > A" -> "As you Go >
Real", shell count 8 -> 5. (First attempt failed because main has no
commands.navigateToFolder — 002's addition; the click now reuses the
pill's own navigation sequence.)

EXPANSION
The existing chevron on the ancestor tile; expanding shows its other
children in place (probed: expanding Real spawns Profiles). One note:
ancestor expansion persists as the real folder's OWN
graphExpandedGroupIds state — it is a real folder, and its expansion
preference is real state, not trail state. Easy to change if BRAIN
disagrees.

SAFETY (standing rule 2) — measured
Ancestors join as ordinary unpinned bodies seeded near the centre:
settles in 244 ticks (~4.1s at 60Hz); pinned (arranged) items move 0px
by mechanism; unpinned items' settle is displaced at most 251.4px
attributable to the ancestors versus the same view settling without
them. Same order as any fresh items arriving in the view.

EVIDENCE — work/captures-003/ (13 PNG + 13 probe txt + nav probe)
Real state.json (read-only) through the real code path. depth1 (in
Real): 1 ancestor (As you Go). mid (in A): 2 (As you Go, Real).
expanded (in A, Real expanded by a real click): 2 ancestors + Profiles
spawned as an ordinary hue-ringed folder. root: 0 (nothing — as
expected). Both themes + transparent; current folder absent in all 13.
Probes verify the ring/fill values above and that real folders keep
their hues.

PRE-EXISTING, NOT MINE (reported, untouched)
1. Two toolbar tests fail on CLEAN main: toolbar-controller.test.mjs
   expects percentage offsets, main's toolbar-controller.js produces px
   ('200px' !== '25%'). The code and tests disagree on main; I changed
   neither (BRAIN's call — it involves the accepted toolbar behavior).
2. fitGraph still throws "viewportSelection.transition is not a
   function" (d3-transition not vendored) — the initial fit silently

3. bin-origin ghosts can be marquee-selected on main (the pattern's
   shipped hole — ghosts are pointer-events:none so pointer paths can't
   touch them, but the marquee reads DOM rects). I closed it for

STATE OF THE TREE

VERDICT
Engineering review passes. Assignment 003 remains open only for the
creator's eye check, which BRAIN cannot substitute for. No commit, push,
release, install, launch or restart is authorized.

WHY IT PASSES
1. The path bodies enter through collectVisible / collectVisibleBin and
   then reuse the existing graph renderer, simulation, icon, edge and
   expansion paths. No trail renderer, overlay, solver or CSS system was
   added.
2. Ancestor colour is exactly --folder-color: var(--text), applied through
   the existing folder-coloured rule. The supplied dark/light captures show
   the existing ring and color-mix fill rather than the rejected solid block.
3. Current-folder exclusion, root behavior, Bin behavior, click navigation,
   expansion, non-persistent floating drag and the two drag-drop deletion
   guards have behavioral tests. A separate depth-three probe with a middle
   ancestor expanded produced one body per id; the identity map absorbs the
   already-present chain member rather than duplicating it.
4. The safety harness reports pinned movement 0px; unpinned bodies settle in
   244 ticks (~4.1 seconds) with maximum 251.4px displacement. This is not a
   flashing opacity/fill/luminance/hue animation; the affected area is the
   ordinary item bodies in the entered folder, moving continuously at the
   simulation's existing settling rate.

CORRECTION TO THE WORKER CHECKPOINT
The current dirty tree has THREE failing tests, not two: the two pre-existing
toolbar percentage/px disagreements plus styles.test.mjs expecting the old
breadcrumb surface while a separate working-tree change hides breadcrumbs.
All 695 other tests pass, including every Assignment 003 test. BRAIN does not
fold that unrelated breadcrumb change into Assignment 003 and does not edit it.

SAVE ERROR DIAGNOSIS (READ-ONLY, OUTSIDE ASSIGNMENT 003)
The message is emitted by Papers' BackpackProjectService.saveStateNow. It
writes state.json.tmp-<main PID>-<UUID>, then calls fs.rename(tempPath,
statePath). Windows returned EPERM at the replacement step. The temp filename
names PID 40692, the one Papers main process; PID 61796 is a renderer child,
not a second main writer. Papers serializes its own saves in memory, so there
is no evidence of two Papers main processes racing.

The direct cause is a transient Windows deny-delete/replace handle on the
existing state.json. ACL and attributes are normal; later saves succeeded and
the temp file was cleaned, so this was not a durable permission problem or
invalid state. Syncthing does not own D:\Letters, Dropbox owns only
C:\Users\admin\Dropbox, and OneDrive owns its Documents\OneDrive root, so none
of those sync clients can be named from present evidence. The exact historical
lock owner cannot be recovered without a ProcMon/handle trace captured during
the event. The host-side resilience gap is definite: the rename has no retry
for transient Windows EPERM/EBUSY/ENOTEMPTY.

BRAIN ASSIGNMENT TO WORKER
Do not change Papers for this diagnosis: a host fix belongs to the canonical
Papers repository and would require separately authorized build/release work.
Verify Assignment 003 without launching or writing creator state; record the
test result and stop for the creator's eye check.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 004 (ACTIVE)
PAPERS SHUTDOWN: DESTROY THE BACKPACK SURFACE BEFORE ITS WINDOW
======================================================================

CREATOR REPORT
Closing Papers produces an Electron main-process error dialog every time:
"TypeError: Object has been destroyed" at BackpackProjectRuntime.hide.
The creator explicitly instructed BRAIN to assign the fix.

THE PROVEN CAUSE
Canonical Papers source:
  D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\PAPERS 3\Papers-3

src/main/index.ts currently calls backpackProjectRuntime.hide() from the
BaseWindow `closed` event. `closed` fires after Electron has destroyed the
BaseWindow. BackpackProjectRuntime.hide() then dereferences
this.window.contentView at src/main/backpacks/backpackProjectRuntime.ts:53,
which deterministically throws "Object has been destroyed". Commit bf15f93
introduced this cleanup call while adding transparent Backpack surfaces.

THE EXPERIENCE TO DELIVER
Closing Papers after entering As you Go closes normally. No JavaScript error
dialog appears. The Backpack child surface is detached and closed before its
parent window is destroyed. Repeated cleanup calls are harmless.

AUTHORIZED SCOPE
- Canonical Papers host source only, limited to the BaseWindow / Backpack
  project-surface lifecycle.
- Add focused behavioral lifecycle tests.
- Run non-launching source checks/tests proportionate to the change.

NOT AUTHORIZED
- No Backpack feature changes.
- No state.json changes.
- Do not include the separate transient state-save rename retry.
- No refactor of other runtime/window lifecycles.
- No commit, push, build, package, release, install, launch or restart.

WORKING-TREE SAFETY
The canonical Papers tree is already dirty with creator/other-worker changes,
including src/main/index.ts plus Settings/bridge/IPC/preload work and new
windowBounds files/tests. Preserve all of it. Inspect the exact overlapping
index.ts hunk before editing. Do not revert, reformat or absorb those changes.

IMPLEMENTATION BOUNDARY
1. Move Backpack surface cleanup to a lifecycle point before BaseWindow native
   destruction (`close`, not `closed`). Keep post-destruction bookkeeping in
   `closed`.
2. Make BackpackProjectRuntime.hide() idempotent and defensive: clear its own
   reference once, do not call methods on a destroyed BaseWindow or destroyed
   WebContents, and remain safe if cleanup is requested twice or arrives late.
3. Do not hide exceptions that are unrelated to already-destroyed Electron
   objects with a broad catch. Express the lifecycle condition explicitly.
4. Add a behavioral unit test covering normal hide, double hide, and late hide
   after the parent/view is already destroyed. If the Electron class is hard to
   instantiate in unit tests, inject or structurally mock the minimal window
   and view boundary; do not settle for a source-text regex.

ACCEPTANCE EVIDENCE
- The focused lifecycle tests pass.
- Typecheck passes.
- Relevant existing main-process/unit tests pass.
- Full test status is reported, with pre-existing failures separated from the
  assignment.
- `git diff --check` passes.
- Worker reports the exact changed files and why each is necessary.

STOP CONDITION
Append WORKER PICKUP before editing. Append WORKER CHECKPOINT after the source
fix and non-launching verification. Then stop. The creator must separately
authorize build/release/install/restart before the running Papers copy can
receive and visually confirm the fix.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 005 (QUEUED AFTER 004)
TRAIL DESCENDANTS: NO SETS, ONE TRAIL OPACITY
======================================================================

CREATOR CORRECTION
"sets never gets rendered in trail to reduce complexities, and the trails
opacity sliders affect expanded trails items too."

checkpoint, commit, build or release.

THE EXPERIENCE
The trail is the entire derived branch shown because of the path to the current
folder:
1. every ancestor body in the path; and
2. every child/descendant revealed by expanding one of those ancestor bodies.

No set is rendered for, around, or because of anything in that derived trail
branch. Trail bodies also do not receive set gravity, containment, exclusion,
ejection or set-scoped gestures. Sets belonging to ordinary content of the
current folder continue unchanged.

The existing Trail opacity slider affects the whole derived trail branch —
ancestor bodies and all items revealed beneath an expanded ancestor. The
slider changes each affected tile as one unit (icon, label and description),
using the existing --graph-trail-opacity preference. It does not take over the
separate connector, set-outline or region opacity controls.

MECHANISM — ONE PROVENANCE FLAG, NOT MORE TRAIL SYSTEMS
The current partial implementation recognizes only `ancestor: true`. That is
why direct ancestors avoid sets and receive opacity, while expanded children
do not. Do not infer trail membership from depth, DOM position, edge shape or
set membership. Carry one explicit derived-provenance flag through the
existing visibility walk.

1. public/graph-model-20260730b.js
   - In collectVisible and collectVisibleBin, mark ancestor entries and every
     item reached by walking an expanded ancestor as `trail: true`.
   - Propagate `trail: true` through every deeper expansion from that branch.
   - Items reached from the ordinary current-folder walk are not trail.
   - Shared shortcut identity can be reached through both paths. Real current-
     folder provenance wins: the one merged body must be `trail: false`, keep
     ordinary opacity and keep its ordinary sets. Add a regression test for
     this precedence rather than relying on walk order.
   - Preserve `ancestor: true` only for the actual path bodies because their
     navigation/selection/deletion rules remain special. Expanded trail
     descendants are trail items, not ancestors.

2. public/workspace-20260730b.js
   - buildCandidate copies the `trail` provenance.

     Keep `ancestor-item` for ancestor-only interaction rules.
   - Replace the current ancestor-only exclusions in setIdsContaining,
     membersOnScreen and ejection with trail-wide exclusions.
   - Audit every set path (eligibility, ring members, gravity, containment,
     overlap/exclusion, ejection, membership picker and set-scoped gestures).
     Route through the existing set membership boundary where possible; do not
     scatter DOM-only exceptions. A trail item is outside the set system for
     this view, not a foreign trespasser that a set should push away.
   - Do not change persisted itemSets. This is presentation/physics provenance
     for the current derived view only.

3. public/styles/items.css
   - Drive opacity from `.trail-item`, not `.ancestor-item`, using the existing
     --graph-trail-opacity variable. No new slider, preference, colour,
     animation or transition.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 005 CORRECTION
SUPPRESS EACH TRAIL-RELATED SET ENTIRELY
======================================================================

CREATOR CORRECTION
The earlier wording about excluding trail ids from set membership was too
narrow. The creator does NOT want a trail-related set to survive as a partial
ring around its non-trail nodes. Exclusion is SET-WIDE, not node-by-node.

AUTHORITATIVE RULE
For the current visible graph, first identify the complete derived trail branch
(ancestors plus everything revealed beneath expanded ancestors). Evaluate each
persisted set with the existing inherited-membership rule. If ANY visible trail
item would belong to that set, the WHOLE SET is trail-related and is suppressed
for this view.

A suppressed set contributes nothing:
- no outline/ring;
- no name glyph or overlap region;
- no interior effect;
- no gravity, containment, separation or overlap force;
- no exclusion/ejection/trespass response;
- no set-scoped expansion gesture or membership-picker affordance from the
  visible graph.

The persisted set and its membership are untouched. When the creator navigates
to a view where none of that set's visible members are trail-derived, it may
render and behave normally again.

PROVENANCE PRECEDENCE
A shared shortcut visible through both the ordinary current-folder path and a
trail path is one ordinary real body (`trail: false`, as already specified).
That dual path alone does not suppress its sets. A set is suppressed only when
at least one of its visible members remains trail-derived after identity merge.

IMPLEMENTATION DIRECTION
Compute one view-local collection of suppressed set ids from the visible graph
items and the existing belongsToSet + ancestorsOfNode rule. Make every set
consumer use the same eligibility boundary. Do not implement this by merely
filtering trail nodes out of membersOnScreen, and do not repeat independent
`candidate.trail` checks across every physics/effect loop: those approaches
allow a partial set to survive and are the defect the creator just corrected.

The earlier Assignment 005 lines saying ordinary visible members of a trail-
related set may still render, or directing node-only exclusion from set paths,
are superseded by this correction. Unrelated sets — sets with no visible trail
member — remain unchanged.

ADDITIONAL REQUIRED TESTS
- A set with one trail member and several ordinary visible members is entirely
  absent, not redrawn around the ordinary subset.
- Its ring, glyph, regions, effects and every force/ejection consumer all see
  the same suppressed-set decision.
- A different set containing only ordinary visible members still renders and
  acts normally in the same view.
- Navigating so the formerly trail-derived member becomes ordinary makes its
  set eligible again without changing persisted itemSets.
- A dual-provenance shared shortcut resolved as ordinary does not suppress its
  set unless another visible trail member belongs to it.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 005 CORRECTION 2 (AUTHORITATIVE)
TRAIL VIEW OMITS SETS; SET DATA IS NEVER DESTROYED
======================================================================

CREATOR CLARIFICATION
"dont render sets in trail view, dont destroy anything."

The preceding Assignment 005 CORRECTION titled "SUPPRESS EACH TRAIL-RELATED
SET ENTIRELY" was BRAIN's misunderstanding and is VOID. Do not implement its
set-wide suppression rule or its additional tests. This correction is the
authoritative interpretation.

THE EXACT RULE
The unit of exclusion is every ITEM in the complete derived trail branch:
- each ancestor/path body; and
- each child and deeper descendant revealed by expanding an ancestor/path
  body.

Those trail-view items do not participate in set presentation or set physics.
For them, render no set ring, glyph, overlap region or interior effect, and
apply no set gravity, containment, separation, exclusion or ejection.

The unit of exclusion is NOT the persisted set. Never delete, suppress, edit,
normalize, prune or rewrite a set or its membership because one of its members
appears in the trail. If Set A has one trail-view item and two ordinary visible
items, the trail item is ignored by the set system in this view, while Set A
may still render and behave normally around the two ordinary items.

Nothing about view.itemSets or state.json changes. Navigating or collapsing
the trail merely changes which visible bodies carry derived `trail` provenance;
the stored set is byte-for-byte untouched.

MECHANISM
Retain the original Assignment 005 provenance mechanism:
- `trail: true` marks ancestors and everything walked from an expanded
  ancestor, recursively;
- ordinary current-folder traversal yields `trail: false`;
- real provenance wins for a merged shared shortcut visible from both paths;
- `ancestor: true` remains narrower and continues to own only the ancestor's
  navigation/selection/deletion behavior;
- `.trail-item`, not `.ancestor-item`, receives the existing Trail opacity.

Feed only non-trail visible ids/bodies into the existing set rendering and
physics boundaries. Prefer one shared view-local non-trail eligibility helper
so ring, glyph, region, effect, force and ejection consumers cannot disagree.
This helper filters the current rendering inputs only; it must never call a
set mutation or persistence function.

AUTHORITATIVE TESTS (SUPERSEDE THE VOID SET-WIDE TESTS)
- ancestor, expanded child and expanded grandchild are all trail provenance;
- ordinary current-folder items are not trail;
- a shared shortcut reached through trail and ordinary paths resolves ordinary;
- Bin expanded-trail descendants follow the same rule;
- when Set A has one trail item and two ordinary visible items, no set visual
  or force touches the trail item, while Set A still renders/acts on the two
  ordinary items;
- all set presentation/physics consumers receive the same non-trail inputs;
- view.itemSets is deep-equal before and after render/provenance computation;
- Trail opacity styles ancestor and expanded descendant tiles via
  `.trail-item`, without animation, while ordinary tiles remain unaffected.

SAFETY AND AUTHORITY
This is an As you Go project-view change only. Do not touch creator state,
Papers source, set membership data or real-item hue assignment. No commit,
push, build, release, install, launch or restart is authorized. Visual
acceptance remains the creator's eye check.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 006 (QUEUED AFTER 005)
RETRY TRANSIENT WINDOWS STATE REPLACEMENT WITHOUT RISKING CREATOR DATA
======================================================================

CREATOR REPORT AND AUTHORITY
Papers repeatedly shows:
  Error invoking remote method 'host:backpack-project:state-save':
  EPERM: operation not permitted, rename
  state.json.tmp-<PID>-<UUID> -> state.json

The creator instructed BRAIN to add the fix as an assignment. Assignment 005
remains active and must checkpoint first. Then WORKER appends Assignment 006
PICKUP before touching canonical Papers source.

PROVEN FACTS
- BackpackProjectService.saveStateNow writes valid JSON to a same-directory
  UUID temp file, then calls fs.rename(tempPath, statePath) exactly once.
- Windows intermittently denies replacement of the existing state.json with
  EPERM while another process briefly holds a deny-delete/replace handle.
- The file has normal attributes/ACL, later writes succeed, saved JSON remains
  valid, and finally cleans the temp file. This is transient replacement
  contention, not invalid state or a permanent permission failure.
- Assignment 004 fixed only shutdown ordering. It deliberately did not touch
  this save path.

THE EXPERIENCE
Ordinary short-lived Windows file locks are invisible to the creator. Papers
keeps the last complete state until it can atomically replace it, retries for a
short bounded interval, and reports an error only if contention persists. No
state is deleted, truncated or copied non-atomically to make the warning go
away.

OWNERSHIP AND SCOPE
Canonical Papers source only:
  D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\PAPERS 3\Papers-3

Correct owner:
  src/main/backpacks/backpackProjectService.ts

Focused behavioral tests belong in:
  tests/unit/backpackProjectService.test.ts

Do not change the As you Go project for Assignment 006. The generic host owns
the atomic state-file replacement seam; this fix must not name As you Go or
alter its state shape.

DATA-SAFETY INVARIANTS
1. Keep write-temp-then-rename in the SAME directory. Atomic replacement is
   the data-integrity boundary.
2. Never unlink/delete/truncate state.json before replacement.
3. Never fall back to copyFile/writeFile over state.json.
4. Keep the prior complete state readable throughout failed attempts.
5. Use a unique temp file per save and keep finally cleanup.
6. Keep the existing per-Backpack save queue. Retry one queued replacement;
   do not start parallel writes or create a second queue.
7. Non-transient errors still surface immediately. Exhausted transient errors
   still surface with their original code/path context.

BOUNDED RETRY MECHANISM
- Extract the replacement operation behind one small helper owned by the
  Backpack project service module.
- Retry only Windows-style transient replacement codes: EPERM, EBUSY and
  ENOTEMPTY. Do not retry validation, JSON, path, binding, disk-full or other
  errors.
- Use short increasing delays with a hard cap and a bounded total wait around
  one second (for example 25, 50, 100, 200 and 400 ms before the final
  attempt). Exact constants may follow an existing repository precedent if
  one exists; report them.
- Inject or isolate the delay/rename boundary so unit tests use no real-time
  sleeps. Do not add a dependency for this.
- A retry that succeeds resolves the original save normally; the creator sees
  no error toast.

REQUIRED BEHAVIORAL TESTS
- EPERM on the first attempts, then success: rename is retried, the new state
  is complete, and the save resolves.
- EBUSY and ENOTEMPTY follow the same bounded path.
- A non-transient error is attempted once and rethrown unchanged.
- Persistent transient failure stops at the exact attempt limit, rethrows the
  final/originally contextual error, preserves the old state byte-for-byte,
  and cleans the temp file.
- A successful retry leaves no temp file.
- Overlapping saves remain serialized and the last requested complete state
  wins even when the first replacement retries.
- A queued save still runs after an earlier save exhausts retries, preserving
  the existing queue-recovery behavior.

WORKING-TREE SAFETY
Canonical Papers is dirty with creator/other-worker source, including the
Assignment 004 shutdown fix and window/settings work. Preserve it all. Inspect
overlapping service/test hunks before editing; do not revert, reformat, commit
or absorb unrelated work.

VERIFICATION AND STOP
- Run the focused BackpackProjectService tests, typecheck, full unit suite and
  git diff --check.
- Append WORKER 006 CHECKPOINT with exact files, retry constants, attempt
  count, test evidence and data-safety proof.
- No state.json edit or destructive reproduction against creator data.
- No commit, push, publish, build, package, release, install, launch or restart
  unless the creator separately authorizes those delivery steps after review.
- Stop for BRAIN review.

same-directory UUID temp file and renames it over state.json once;
Windows intermittently EPERMs that replacement. I extract the
replacement behind one module-owned helper that retries only EPERM/
EBUSY/ENOTEMPTY with increasing delays (25/50/100/200/400 ms — a bounded
~775 ms total wait, one initial attempt plus five retries), rethrows
non-transient errors immediately, and rethrows exhausted transient
errors with their original code/path context. The rename and delay are
constructor-injected so unit tests run with no real-time sleeps and no
new dependency. Data-safety invariants hold by construction: same-dir
temp+rename only, never unlink/truncate/copy-over state.json, prior
state readable throughout, unique temp per save with finally cleanup,
the existing per-Backpack queue untouched, no parallel writes.

TESTS PLANNED (behavioral, injected boundary)
EPERM-then-success with recorded delays; EBUSY and ENOTEMPTY on the same
path; non-transient attempted once and rethrown as the same error
instance; persistent failure at exactly six attempts with the recorded
[25,50,100,200,400] delays, old state byte-for-byte preserved and temp
cleaned; successful retry leaves no temp file; overlapping saves
serialized with last-wins while the first retries; a queued save still
persists after an earlier save exhausts retries.

BOUNDARIES
No As you Go change, no state.json edit, no destructive reproduction
against creator data, no commit/push/build/package/release/install/
launch/restart. Preserve every pre-existing dirty change (004 shutdown
fix, window/settings work).

IN PLAIN TERMS
Trail expansion is now a second, fully independent expansion system,
remembered per specific view. Ordinary graph expansion and trail
expansion never seed, read or mutate each other. A view with no saved
trail choice starts with every trail folder collapsed; expanding a trail
folder reveals only its direct children; returning to the same view
restores its trail choices; another view remembers its own.

SERIALIZED SHAPE
One narrow map under view: `view.trailExpandedByContext`, mapping an
explicit context key to a unique array of folder ids:
  { "folder:root": [...], "folder:<id>": [...], "bin:bin": [...], "bin:<id>": [...] }
Context-key rule: explorer views use `folder:<id>` (root = `folder:root`);
Bin views use `bin:<id>` (Bin top = `bin:bin`) — normal and Bin views can
never collide. No saved entry = collapsed; an empty list deletes the key.
Legacy state without the field normalizes to `{}` (collapsed everywhere).
Unknown view fields are preserved; malformed keys (anything not
folder:/bin:-prefixed), non-array values, duplicate and non-string ids
are discarded; ids that are no longer valid folders are pruned while the
`root`/`bin` pseudo heads are kept.

   provenance-appropriate set; the [data-expand] click and the
   shift/alt+right-click gestures route trail tiles to the trail-context
   set and ordinary tiles to ordinary expansion.
5. Tests — model.test.mjs: five new tests (Apps-expanded-at-root stays

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 007 (QUEUED AFTER 006)
TRAIL EXPANSION IS INDEPENDENT AND REMEMBERED PER VIEW
======================================================================

CREATOR CORRECTION
A folder's ordinary graph expansion must never leak into the trail. Example:
Apps is expanded at root. In another folder's trail, expanding As you Go may
reveal Apps, but Apps starts COLLAPSED there regardless of its root state. Its
children appear only after the creator explicitly expands Apps in that trail.

The creator clarified that an explicit trail expansion is remembered FOR THAT
SPECIFIC VIEW. Returning to the same view restores its trail choices. A
different view has an independent trail-expansion state and defaults every
trail folder to collapsed.

QUEUE DISCIPLINE
Assignment 006 remains active. WORKER must checkpoint 006 before appending
Assignment 007 PICKUP. Assignment 007 changes only the As you Go project; do
not mix it into the canonical Papers state-save retry diff or checkpoint.

AUTHORITATIVE BEHAVIOR
For each view context, there are two unrelated expansion systems:
1. ordinary graph expansion for ordinary content; and
2. trail expansion for bodies carrying derived `trail: true` provenance.

Rules:
- Never fall back to ordinary graphExpandedGroupIds when deciding whether a
  trail folder is open.
- A view with no saved trail-specific choice starts with an EMPTY trail
  expansion set: every trail folder is collapsed.
- Expanding one trail folder reveals only its direct children. Any revealed
  child folder is also collapsed unless explicitly expanded in this same
  trail/view context.
- Toggling a trail folder changes only this view's trail expansion set.
- Toggling an ordinary folder changes only ordinary expansion.
- Returning to the same folder view restores that view's trail expansion set.
- Another folder view starts from and remembers a different set.
- Normal explorer and Bin views cannot collide: use explicit context keys such
  as `folder:<id>` and `bin:<id>`, including root/Bin pseudo heads.
- Reopening Papers restores the remembered per-view trail choices. Legacy
  state without this field defaults safely to empty everywhere.

OWNERSHIP AND DATA SHAPE
This is durable As you Go view-preference data under this project's existing
`view` state. Do not manually edit state.json and do not change Papers source.
Add one narrow map (for example `trailExpandedByContext`) from explicit view-
context key to a unique array of folder ids. Preserve unknown fields; discard
malformed keys/values; prune ids that are no longer valid folders while
preserving `root` and `bin` pseudo heads where meaningful. Do not create a
general expansion framework.

MECHANISM
1. Store/model
   - Add pure normalization and update operations for the per-context trail
     expansion map.
   - The session exposes the active trail-expanded set for the current explicit
     context. Persistence runs through the existing store/save queue.

2. Visibility walk
   - Pass ordinary-expanded and active-trail-expanded sets separately into
     visibleGraphItems / collectVisible / collectVisibleBin.
   - The ordinary current-folder walk consults only ordinary expansion.
   - Every branch with `trail: true` consults only active trail expansion,
     recursively. Never consult ordinary expansion while walking a trail.
   - Preserve Assignment 005 provenance: real provenance wins for an identity
     reached through both ordinary and trail paths.

3. Affordances
   - On create and update, a folder's chevron state comes from the expansion
     set matching its candidate provenance.
   - Chevron and supported right-click expansion gestures route trail tiles to
     the active trail-context set and ordinary tiles to ordinary expansion.
   - `ancestor: true` remains only the navigation/deletion distinction;
     expanded trail descendants use the same trail expansion mechanism.
   - Assignment 005 remains intact: trail items stay outside set rendering and
     physics and share Trail opacity.

WHAT NOT TO DO
- Do not reset trail state merely because the graph rerenders.
- Do not use one global trail-expanded set: remembrance is per specific view.
- Do not copy ordinary expansion into a new trail context.
- Do not make trail expansion alter ordinary expansion or vice versa.
- Do not change trail set/opacity behavior, real-item hues, graph solver,
  Papers source or the window-workspace planning.

REQUIRED BEHAVIORAL TESTS
- Apps expanded in the root/ordinary set still appears collapsed when first
  revealed inside a trail.
- Expanding As you Go reveals direct children but does not recursively expand
  Apps from its ordinary state.
- Explicitly expanding Apps in trail context `folder:Real` reveals its children
  and survives rerender, navigation away/back and state normalize/reload.
- The same Apps trail body defaults collapsed in another context, then can be
  remembered independently there.
- Collapsing Apps outside does not change either trail context; toggling either
  trail context does not change ordinary graphExpandedGroupIds.
- A deeper trail descendant uses trail expansion recursively.
- Normal and Bin context keys do not collide.
- Legacy/malformed state normalizes to safe collapsed defaults without losing
  unrelated view data.
- Create/update chevrons and every supported expansion gesture display and
  mutate the provenance-appropriate state.
- Assignment 005 provenance, set exclusion and opacity tests remain green.

VERIFICATION AND STOP
- Focused model/store/renderer/interaction tests, then npm test.
- Report pre-existing dirty-tree failures separately; run git diff --check.
- Append WORKER 007 CHECKPOINT with exact files, serialized shape, context-key
  rule and evidence that ordinary/trail/per-view states cannot leak.
- No manual state.json edit. No commit, push, Papers build/release/install,
  launch or restart. Stop for BRAIN review and creator eye check.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - WINDOW-LAYOUT ITEM ACCEPTANCE ROADMAP
SMALL PROOFS ON THE FINAL ARCHITECTURE
======================================================================

CREATOR'S DESTINATION
Create a genuine new As you Go workspace item type that represents one saved
group of desktop windows. Many such items may exist. Each item shows a small
spatial map of its member windows, like the creator's illustration.

The first creator-acceptable milestone must do all of the following:
- The window-layout object is a REAL persisted workspace item type, not a Tool,
  toolbar, overlay or special object outside the graph.
- It obeys the same workspace rules as other real items: folder ownership,
  graph position/physics, selection, background dragging, sets, Bin/restore,
  copy/duplicate, visibility, navigation, theme and save/reload.
- It can explicitly CAPTURE the current desktop bounds/state of its chosen
  windows as a saved arrangement. Live movement updates the miniature but
  NEVER silently overwrites the saved arrangement.
- Each miniature window icon reflects open/minimized/maximized/closed-or-missing
  state and current relative screen position.
- Clicking one miniature icon toggles that external window between minimized
  and restored/open. An item-level minus minimizes all members. An item-level
  square restores/opens all members AND reapplies the deliberately saved
  arrangement.
- Moving, resizing, minimizing, maximizing, opening or closing a member window
  updates the item without requiring the As you Go graph to be rebuilt.
- Missing or ambiguous windows are reported on the relevant miniature entry;
  they do not make the whole item fail and never cause an unrelated window to
  be controlled.
- No command in this feature closes or destroys an external window.

NOT PART OF THE FIRST ACCEPTANCE MILESTONE
Detaching the item outside Papers, reattaching it, or keeping a detached item
alive after Papers exits. Those remain the intended later direction, but only
after the in-workspace item and native control boundary have proved reliable.
Do not build a companion process yet.

ARCHITECTURE BOUNDARIES
1. `window-layout` is a distinct domain kind. Do not disguise it as a shortcut,
   folder, set or toolbar, and do not encode its data in graphPositions.
2. Reuse the existing generic graph pipeline. Extend its item-kind seams once;
   do not clone renderer, simulation, selection, pointer, set, Bin or trail
   systems. A new kind may supply its own tile contents and intrinsic footprint,
   but the ordinary workspace owns the shell and movement.
3. Persist stable intent only: item id/name/parent/order/bin metadata, member
   matching/launch descriptors and explicitly captured arrangements. Never
   persist HWNDs or treat a window title alone as durable identity.
4. Live window observations are runtime state, outside persisted project state.
   The item renderer consumes observations; it does not own enumeration or
   native commands.
5. All desktop-window access sits behind one narrow, capability-shaped host
   adapter. The As you Go model and renderer must be fully testable with a fake
   adapter. Do not expose arbitrary process execution or arbitrary host IPC to
   Backpack content.
6. Saved arrangement and live observation are separate values. Capture is the
   only operation that replaces saved bounds. Restore reads saved bounds.
7. Native identity resolution must fail closed: zero or multiple safe matches
   means missing/ambiguous, not "pick the first window."
8. Reuse the item's ordinary workspace id everywhere that rules are keyed by
   item identity (positions, selection, set membership, Bin and trail). Do not
   introduce a shadow graph id.
9. Derived `trail: true` instances inherit Assignment 005/007 behavior: trail
   opacity, no set rendering/physics in that view, and per-view trail expansion.
   Their presence must not duplicate the persisted window-layout record.
10. No large-area animation, flashing opacity/fill/luminance/hue, or polling
    that rebuilds/reheats graph physics. Status updates should patch only the
    small item interior.

PROOF SEQUENCE AND GATES

prove persistence and ALL ordinary workspace rules without native control.
Proof B: native host feasibility behind the final narrow adapter, controlling
only a disposable test window after explicit creator launch authorization.

Each proof gets focused behavioral tests, full relevant suites and a WORKER
checkpoint. BRAIN reviews evidence and narrows the next proof. Do not jump to

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - CREATOR UX DIRECTION
SESSION-FIRST LIVE LAYOUTS, QUICK PICKING AND MEMBER OPERATIONS
======================================================================

This entry supersedes the earlier assumption that the ordinary use case is a
carefully named, manually captured static arrangement.

PLAIN MODEL
- A new layout is lightweight and session-first. The creator expects to make
  several rapidly, use them for roughly a session, then discard most of them.
- Ease of creating, filling, rearranging and deleting a layout is therefore
  more important than an elaborate preset editor.
- Layout deletion removes only the layout item and its membership/geometry. It
  NEVER closes member windows by default.
- A separate explicit, confirmed "close member windows" choice may exist. It
  acts only on windows resolved as members at that moment and fails closed on
  missing/ambiguous identity. Deleting and closing must not be conflated.
- If a layout proves useful, the creator can explicitly KEEP it. Do not solve
  the full kept-layout lifecycle in 009 or the first one-window slice.

LIVE STATE VERSUS PER-LAYOUT GEOMETRY
Every layout containing a window may display that window's current open/
minimized/maximized/missing state. Each layout nevertheless owns a DIFFERENT
saved bounds/state entry for that membership, so the same window may appear in
multiple layouts at different positions and sizes.

To prevent one physical move from silently overwriting every alternate layout,
only ONE layout is active/recording at a time:
- Creating or activating a session layout makes it the recording layout.
- While active, adding/removing members and moving/resizing member windows
  continuously updates that layout's current arrangement; there is no Save
  Positions ceremony for the ordinary session workflow.
- Inactive layouts still observe live status but retain their own geometry.
- Activating another layout applies its retained geometry, restores/opens the
  requested members, and transfers recording to that layout.
- Kept/frozen behavior will later add an explicit choice; do not let it muddy
  the live session proof. Persist live edits promptly for crash recovery, but
  do not invent automatic shutdown deletion before a creator-approved policy.

MEMBER PICKER
- A layout offers "Pick onscreen windows." The picker lists currently visible,
  eligible top-level windows with the program icon by default and enough title/
  application detail to distinguish them.
- Picking an absent member links it; picking an existing member again unlinks
  it. A layout contains a given resolved window identity at most once.
- The same window may be linked to many layouts. Runtime observation should be
  shared/deduplicated, while membership and geometry remain per-layout.
- Picker resolution fails closed on ambiguity and excludes Papers/internal,
  tool, invisible and unsafe system windows according to explicit rules.
- Manual member-button order is independent of desktop position and persists
  per layout. Reordering never changes window bounds.

MEMBER SELECTION AND ACTIONS
- Member selection lives INSIDE the layout item and is separate from ordinary
  workspace-item selection. A normal member click may retain the eventual
  minimize/restore toggle; Ctrl-click (or an equally clear tested affordance)
  toggles member multiselection without starting graph drag.
- Reorder is a deliberate member drag/handle interaction and must not move the
  outer workspace item. Background drag continues to move the layout item.
- Group actions apply to selected members when any are selected, otherwise to
  every member. Required actions: restore/open, minimize and isolate.
- Isolate means restore/open the selected members and minimize the UNSELECTED
  members OF THIS LAYOUT only. It never changes windows outside this layout.
- Opening a closed member requires safe stored launch identity; an already-open
  member is restored. Missing/ambiguous members remain reported, never guessed.
- Default member visuals use the owning program's icon; status is a small,
  static semantic indicator with no repeating animation.

ARCHITECTURE CONSEQUENCES
- Persist per-layout ordered memberships and per-membership geometry, not HWNDs.
- Keep live observations/runtime handles in one host-side registry so duplicate
  membership across layouts does not create duplicate polling/hooks.
- Maintain an explicit active-recording layout id separately from each layout's
  contents. Only that id is allowed to write movement/resizing observations back
  into geometry.
- UI updates patch member interiors/status and must not rebuild or reheat graph
  physics.
- 008's explicit Save Positions fixture control is no longer the primary flow.
  It may be removed or later repurposed for kept/frozen layouts; Proof C must
  not require it for an ordinary session layout.

======================================================================
[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - UX ADDENDUM: DRAG MEMBER BUTTONS OUT
======================================================================

AUTHORITATIVE INTERACTION
- Dragging a member button WITHIN its layout reorders that layout's persisted
  member order. It does not move or resize the external window.
- Dragging a member button OUTSIDE the layout's member area unlinks that window
  from this layout. It does not close, minimize, move or otherwise alter the
  external window, and it does not remove the same window from other layouts.
- The unlink commits only after a clear outside-drop threshold; cancel/Escape or
  dropping back inside leaves membership unchanged. Provide a restrained static
  removal target/preview, never a flashing large-area effect.
- Inner member dragging must never move the outer workspace item. Background
  dragging still moves the complete window-layout item through ordinary graph
  physics.

SCOPE CLARITY
This is member unlink/reorder behavior for a later product slice, not Assignment
009's native feasibility harness. The earlier whole-layout detachment direction
also remains later work. Do not interpret this addendum as a request to make
individual member buttons into detached desktop controllers.

======================================================================
[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - UX CLARIFICATION: PER-LAYOUT WINDOW STATE
======================================================================

AUTHORITATIVE EXAMPLE
If Layout 1 contains Window A at state X and Layout 2 contains the SAME Window A
at state Y, activating Layout 1 must resume A at X; activating Layout 2 must
resume A at Y. Neither layout's stored state overwrites the other's.

MEANING OF PER-LAYOUT STATE
Each membership owns its layout-specific geometry/state: monitor/work area,
position, size and normal/minimized/maximized intent as supported safely. The
runtime window identity may be shared, but its arrangement is not global.

ACTIVATION RULE
- Resolve each member fail-closed; safely launch a closed member only when its
  stored launch identity is sufficient.
- Apply the activated layout's own retained state to every requested member.
- Make that layout the sole active/recording layout after application.
- Subsequent user moves/resizes/state changes update only that active layout's
  membership entries. Inactive layouts may show live status but retain their
  independent stored arrangements.
- Switching layouts first stops recording into the old layout, applies the new
  layout, then records only into the new one. Programmatic application must not
  echo back into or corrupt another layout.

This requirement belongs to the later product slices; Assignment 009 must make
its recommended capability boundary able to read and apply the necessary state,
but must not implement multi-layout activation itself.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 010
PAPERS-SIDE TYPED WINDOW CLIENT/SUPERVISOR SEAM — FAKE TRANSPORT ONLY
======================================================================

PURPOSE
Prove the product-side architecture that will own the native helper without yet
spawning a process or connecting Backpack content. This isolates protocol and
lifecycle correctness from native launch and UX.

REPOSITORY AND DIRTY-TREE BOUNDARY
Assignment 010 changes canonical Papers source/tests only:
`D:\Letters\MatTroiSeConMoc\PAPERS 3\Papers-3`.
That tree already contains creator/previous-assignment changes. Preserve them.
Prefer new narrowly named module/test files; do not edit existing dirty host,
preload, IPC, settings, runtime or state-save files in this proof. If a necessary
seam overlaps them, checkpoint the conflict and stop rather than absorbing it.

TYPED CONTRACT
Define a narrow internal capability vocabulary sufficient for later slices:
- list eligible top-level window observations;
- observe one already resolved ephemeral runtime id;
- minimize, restore, apply bounds/state and graceful-close one resolved member;
- opaque runtime ids are live-session values and are structurally separate from
  any future persisted matching/launch descriptor;
- explicit typed outcomes for success, missing, ambiguous, denied, malformed,
  helper-unavailable and timeout. Never convert zero/multiple into a guessed id.

CLIENT / SUPERVISOR SEAM
- Client owns request ids, schema validation, bounded timeouts and correlation.
- Inject a transport interface. Tests use an in-memory fake only; no child
  process, PowerShell, native window, socket, global hook or polling loop.
- Supervisor owns abstract start/ready/stop/crash states and rejects pending
  requests deterministically on crash/stop. It must not silently replay a move,
  minimize, restore or close command after restart.
- Ignore/reject malformed, duplicate, stale and mismatched responses without
  satisfying the wrong request. Bound any pending-request collection.
- Expose enumerated capability methods, not raw protocol send, arbitrary Win32,
  shell execution or arbitrary process launch.

SECURITY / PRODUCT BOUNDARY
This remains main-process-internal. Do not add hostFacade, IPC, preload, parent
postMessage or Backpack methods yet. Do not let a renderer supply raw HWNDs,
executables, scripts or command lines. Graceful close remains a distinct future
confirmed operation, never a side effect of layout deletion.

REQUIRED TESTS
- request/response correlation with out-of-order replies;
- timeout and bounded pending cleanup;
- malformed/unknown/duplicate/stale/mismatched messages fail closed;
- stop/crash rejects every pending request exactly once;
- restart does not replay mutations;
- typed missing/ambiguous/denied/helper-unavailable outcomes survive transport;
- allowed method/command union contains no arbitrary execute/send escape hatch;
- runtime ids are absent from any persisted descriptor type;
- fake list/observe/minimize/restore/apply/close route to only the requested id.

NOT IN 010
No native/helper spawn, no PowerShell execution, no dependency install, no
existing host bridge/preload/IPC edit, no As you Go change, no picker, no item
button wiring, no persistence, no polling, no build/package/install/launch/
restart and no creator state edit.

VERIFICATION AND STOP
Run focused new tests, complete Papers unit suite, typecheck and diff check.
Append WORKER 010 CHECKPOINT to this coordination log with exact files, public
internal types, state machine, evidence and dirty-tree separation. Do not commit
or push. Stop for BRAIN review; BRAIN creates the reviewed snapshot commit before
dispatching the helper-transport proof.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] WORKER - Assignment 009 PICKUP

installation, restart, Papers modification, As you Go state change, 008

ENVIRONMENT FACTS (checked before pick-up)
- PowerShell 7.6.3, Windows 10.0.26200 (AMD64), System.Windows.Forms
  loads in-process (no install).
- Route comparison conclusion: direct Win32 via Add-Type P/Invoke in
  PS 7 is the only viable zero-install route here. A Node native addon
  would need node-gyp + a compiler toolchain; a maintained npm native
  window package would need an install (reporting, not installing); the
  installed PowerToys Workspaces executables are internal and not a
  documented/stable API (per the contract, not treated as one).

DELIVERABLE PLAN (isolated probe, not production runtime)
Location: <repo>/probes/native-window/
1. window-capability.ps1 — one narrow typed adapter: enum visible
   top-level windows (ephemeral runtime id, pid, process path, title,
   bounds, normal/minimized/maximized), Resolve-Target fail-closed on
   0/multiple, Get/Set bounds, Minimize/Restore, IsIconic/IsZoomed,
   graceful close for an already resolved member (addendum). The window
   source is injectable so fake tests can prove fail-closed matching and
   command routing without any live window.
2. window-capability.test.ps1 — deterministic fake tests: zero-match
   and multi-match resolution reject; command routing never falls
   through to a different id; bounds/state commands round-trip; the
   close contract refuses anything not resolved first; no exec-style
   escape hatch exists.
3. run-feasibility.ps1 — live transcript: launch ONE WinForms window
   with a unique title; enumerate and resolve it; record original
   bounds/state; minimize → verify IsIconic; restore → verify; set new
   bounds → read back; close it → disappearance reported and graceful
   close proven; cleanup asserts no window with the unique title
   remains and the process exited.
Safety: only the harness-launched target is ever manipulated or closed;
original bounds/state recorded before each operation; nothing persists.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 010 REVIEW: CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
Do not commit the 010 snapshot yet. File isolation, method vocabulary, bounded
pending map and basic supervisor behavior pass, and BRAIN reran the focused 14
tests plus typecheck successfully. The fail-closed schema/correlation boundary
is incomplete in ways the current tests do not cover.

FINDING 1 — NESTED PAYLOADS ARE CAST, NOT VALIDATED
parseWindowResponse validates the envelope but accepts `windows` as any array
and `observation` as any object (including arrays), then casts them to trusted
WindowObservation types. A malformed helper response can therefore inject bad
runtime ids, states, bounds or process fields through the supposedly typed gate.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- requestId must be a positive safe integer.
- A runtime id must be a non-empty string at the wire boundary.
- Validate every WindowObservation field: title string; processId null or a
  non-negative safe integer; processPath null or string; allowed WindowState;
  bounds null or a plain object with finite x/y/width/height and positive width/
  height. Reject arrays and malformed nested entries.
- If `windows` or `observation` is present, validate it deeply. Do not return a
  partially filtered payload and do not use an unchecked cast as validation.
- Enforce outcome/method payload shapes sufficiently that a successful `list`
  cannot omit/forge its list and a successful observation/mutation cannot pass
  malformed observation data. A successful `close` may use the explicitly
  documented no-observation shape.

FINDING 2 — SAME-METHOD WRONG-TARGET RESPONSE CAN RESOLVE A REQUEST
PendingEntry stores only the method. A request to observe/minimize/etc. Window A
can be satisfied by a response carrying a valid observation for Window B as long
as requestId and method match. That violates "route only to the requested id."

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Store the request target in PendingEntry.
- For every target method whose response contains an observation, require its
  runtimeId to equal the pending target before resolving. A mismatch is ignored
  fail-closed and the real request remains pending.
- Add a regression with two runtime ids proving an A request cannot resolve from
  a B observation, then resolves only from A. Cover close's documented response
  shape separately.

FINDING 3 — TRANSPORT CONSTRUCTION FAILURE STRANDS `starting`
If createTransport or client subscription throws, start() leaves the supervisor
in `starting`. A real spawn/handshake seam must fail into `crashed`, expose no
client and retain no transport/client references.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Catch construction/subscription failure, best-effort close any constructed
  transport, clear references, transition to `crashed`, and fail closed.
- Define whether start() rejects or resolves in that case and test the chosen
  contract. A later start must use a fresh transport and send/replay nothing.

REQUIRED REGRESSIONS
- NaN/infinite/fractional/negative request ids; array observation; malformed
  runtime id/state/process fields/bounds; one bad entry invalidates a windows
  array; successful list missing windows; success mutation malformed payload.
- same-method wrong-target response cannot resolve A with B.
- factory throw and onMessage/subscription throw leave crashed/no client; fresh
  restart works and replays nothing; partial transport closes once.
- Existing 14 tests and all Papers tests/typecheck remain green.

BOUNDARY AND STOP
Change only the four new 010 files and its new test. Preserve every pre-existing
dirty Papers file. No helper spawn, PowerShell, native window, IPC/preload/host
bridge, As you Go feature/state, dependency, build/install/launch or commit.
Append WORKER 010R CHECKPOINT with exact validation predicates and evidence,
then stop for BRAIN re-review.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 011 DISPATCH
======================================================================

GOAL — ONE SMALL ARCHITECTURE PROOF
Add a Papers-side JSON-lines transport implementation for the accepted
WindowTransport seam, tested only with injected readable/writable fakes. This
assignment proves framing, bounded memory, backpressure and terminal lifecycle.
It does not start or choose a helper and it does not control any real window.

ALLOWED SCOPE
- Prefer new files under src/main/windows/ and one focused unit test file.
- If required, minimally extend only the four committed 010 files. Explain why.
- Preserve every pre-existing dirty Papers file byte-for-byte and unstaged.
- Use platform-neutral injected stream-like interfaces narrow enough to fake;
  do not bind the capability client to Node child-process objects.

REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
1. Outbound messages serialize as UTF-8 JSON followed by exactly one newline.
   `send()` returns a promise reflecting acceptance/backpressure: it must not
   report success before a false/backpressured write has drained, and it must
   reject on write failure or terminal closure.
2. Inbound bytes support arbitrary chunk boundaries, multiple coalesced lines,
   LF and CRLF, and UTF-8 characters split across chunks. Deliver each complete
   decoded line once, in order, to the existing client message boundary.
3. Parse each line as JSON and require the existing response schema gate before
   delivery. Malformed JSON/schema is fail-closed: it satisfies no request and
   cannot expose an untyped payload. Define whether one malformed line is
   ignored or terminal, document the choice, and test it.
4. Set explicit, small configurable maximums for one encoded line and for the
   unfinished receive buffer. Reject/terminate before memory can grow without
   bound. Count encoded bytes, not JavaScript character count. Test exact limit,
   limit+1, and an unterminated oversized stream.
5. EOF, read error, write error and explicit close are terminal and idempotent.
   Close the underlying endpoints at most once; no later messages are emitted;
   pending client requests resolve helper-unavailable through the existing
   supervisor lifecycle, exactly once.
6. A transport terminal event must be observable by the supervisor so it enters
   crashed/unavailable. Extend the narrow seam only as needed; retain clean stop
   as stopped. A fresh restart creates a fresh transport/client and replays no
   request.
7. Expose no raw product-side send/exec/shell/spawn/eval escape hatch. The
   capability client remains the only six-method command vocabulary.

MANDATORY REGRESSIONS
- fragmented UTF-8 and JSON; multiple lines in one chunk; CRLF/LF mixture;
- malformed JSON and schema; empty line policy;
- outbound escaping/newline and synchronous/asynchronous write failure;
- backpressure waits for drain, then resolves; close/error while waiting rejects;
- byte limit exact boundary, limit+1, oversized unterminated buffer;
- EOF/error/explicit close happen once, ignore later chunks, and supervisor
  rejects pending once as helper-unavailable;
- restart is fresh and sends/replays nothing.

OUT OF SCOPE / FORBIDDEN
No child process, PowerShell invocation, executable discovery, socket, named
pipe, HWND/native call, polling, hook, dependency, renderer, As you Go change,
hostFacade/IPC/preload/postMessage wiring, persistence, UI, build/package,
install, launch/restart, commit or push. Do not touch creator state.json.

EVIDENCE AND STOP
Run the focused tests, full Papers unit suite, `npm run typecheck`, and
`git diff --check`. List exact changed files and prove the earlier dirty tree
was untouched. Append `WORKER - Assignment 011 CHECKPOINT` with the chosen
malformed-line policy, byte limits, lifecycle state/evidence and test counts,
then stop without committing. BRAIN will review and create the snapshot commit.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] WORKER - Assignment 011 PICKUP
======================================================================

UNDERSTOOD
A Papers-side JSON-lines transport over injected stream-like fakes only;
nothing is spawned and no real window is controlled.

CHOSEN POLICIES (documented here, tested in the checkpoint)
- Malformed-line policy: IGNORE. A line that is empty, fails JSON.parse,
  or fails the 010R deep schema gate is dropped — it satisfies no
  request, never exposes an untyped payload, and does not kill the
  session (a helper may emit stray output without being dead).
- Byte limits: maxLineBytes default 64 KiB and maxReceiveBufferBytes
  default 256 KiB, both counting ENCODED BYTES. A line that exceeds the
  line cap, or an unfinished buffer that exceeds the buffer cap, is
  TERMINAL (fail closed) — memory cannot grow unbounded. The outbound
  line cap rejects the send without killing the transport.
- Terminal lifecycle: EOF, read error, write error and explicit close
  are all terminal and idempotent; endpoints close at most once; no
  later messages are emitted; pending client requests resolve
  helper-unavailable exactly once.
- Supervisor observability: the transport reports unexpected terminal
  events via an OPTIONAL onTerminal method added to the WindowTransport
  seam (the only extension to the 010 files, explained in the
  checkpoint); the supervisor enters crashed, nulls the client and
  rejects pendings exactly once. Clean stop stays stopped.

FILES
- NEW src/main/windows/jsonLineTransport.ts — createJsonLineTransport
  over narrow injected TransportSink (write returns boolean|Promise for
  backpressure, onDrain, end) and TransportSource (onChunk, onEnd, end).
  Outbound: TextEncoder UTF-8 JSON + '\n', send() waits for drain after
  a false write, rejects on write failure/terminal. Inbound: byte-level
  line framing (LF, CRLF stripped), UTF-8 split across chunks decodes
  per complete line, delivery through the 010R schema gate.
- MINIMAL 010 extensions (why in the checkpoint): windowCapabilityTypes
  .ts gains optional `onTerminal`; windowCapabilitySupervisor.ts
  subscribes and handles unexpected terminal (crashed, pendings rejected
  once, no double close).
- NEW tests/unit/jsonLineTransport.test.ts with FakeSink/FakeSource.

REGRESSIONS COVERED: fragmented UTF-8/JSON; multi-line chunks; LF/CRLF
mixture; malformed JSON/schema; empty lines; outbound escaping/newline;
sync + async write failure; backpressure waits for drain then resolves;
close/error while waiting rejects; byte limits exact/limit+1/oversized
unterminated; EOF/error/close once + later chunks ignored + supervisor
rejects pending once; fresh restart replays nothing.

BOUNDARIES: no spawn/PowerShell/socket/pipe/HWND/polling/hook/
dependency/renderer/As you Go/hostFacade/IPC/preload/persistence/UI/
build/install/launch/commit. No state.json touch.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] WORKER - Assignment 011 CHECKPOINT
======================================================================

EXACT FILES
- NEW src/main/windows/jsonLineTransport.ts — createJsonLineTransport
  over injected TransportSink (write -> boolean|Promise for backpressure,
  onDrain, end) and TransportSource (onChunk, onEnd, end); platform-
  neutral, nothing bound to Node child-process objects.
- NEW tests/unit/jsonLineTransport.test.ts — 15 tests over FakeSink/
  FakeSource.
- MINIMALLY EXTENDED (why): windowCapabilityTypes.ts gains ONE optional
  method on the committed WindowTransport seam — onTerminal(callback) —
  the only way the supervisor can observe an unexpected helper terminal
  without polling; windowCapabilitySupervisor.ts subscribes to it: a

  stays `stopped`. The committed client and its test file were not
  changed.

CHOSEN POLICIES (documented in code and tests)
- Malformed-line policy: IGNORE. Empty lines, invalid JSON, and lines
  failing the 010R deep schema gate are dropped — they satisfy no
  request, never expose an untyped payload, and do not kill the session
  (a helper may emit stray output without being dead). Tested.
- Byte limits: maxLineBytes 64 KiB and maxReceiveBufferBytes 256 KiB,
  both counting ENCODED BYTES (byte-level framing; TextDecoder decodes
  only complete lines). A line over the line cap, or an unfinished
  buffer over the buffer cap, is TERMINAL (fail closed) — memory cannot
  grow without bound. The outbound line cap rejects only that send.
- Terminal lifecycle: EOF, read error, write error and explicit close
  are terminal and idempotent; sink/source end at most once; no later
  messages are emitted; drain waiters are rejected; pending client
  requests resolve helper-unavailable exactly once through the
  supervisor.

LIFECYCLE EVIDENCE
- Fragmented UTF-8/JSON byte-by-byte delivery; a multi-byte character
  split across chunks round-trips; multiple coalesced lines + LF/CRLF
  mixture deliver in order; empty/malformed/schema-invalid lines are
  ignored and the valid close line still delivers.
- Outbound: UTF-8 JSON + exactly one newline; sync and async write
  failures reject the send and are terminal exactly once; backpressured
  send does not resolve before drain, resolves on drain, and rejects
  when the transport terminates while waiting; sends after terminal
  reject.
- Byte limits: exact limit accepted, limit+1 terminal, an unterminated
  oversized stream terminal, outbound over-limit rejects without
  killing the transport.
- EOF / read error / explicit close: terminal once, later chunks and
  repeated terminal events ignored, endpoints closed exactly once.
- Supervisor integration: EOF on the source crashes the supervisor
  (state `crashed`, getClient() null) and the pending minimize resolves
  helper-unavailable exactly once; clean stop stays `stopped`; a fresh
  restart sends nothing.

EVIDENCE COUNTS
- Focused transport suite: 15/15. Capability suite (010R): 21/21.
- Full Papers unit suite: 22 files / 193 tests, ALL pass.
- npm run typecheck: passes. git diff --check: clean (exit 0).

DIRTY-TREE PROOF
Only the four committed 010 files could be touched; two were minimally
extended (types: one optional onTerminal; supervisor: subscription +
terminal handling) and two were not (client, its test). All earlier
dirty Papers files (SettingsPane, bridge, backpackProjectRuntime/
Service, hostFacade, index, hostIpc, preload/host, hostPreload tests,
windowBounds) remain byte-for-byte untouched and unstaged.

BOUNDARY AND STOP

native call, polling, hook, dependency, renderer, As you Go change,
