[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 011 REVIEW: CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
Do not commit the Papers snapshot yet. The ordinary framing/schema paths are
well isolated and BRAIN reran the focused 36 tests, full 22-file / 193-test
suite and typecheck successfully. Three terminal/backpressure boundaries remain
unsafe or incompletely specified; the passing tests do not exercise them.

FINDING 1 — NEW SUPERVISOR SUBSCRIPTION IS OUTSIDE START FAILURE GUARD
`constructed.onTerminal(...)` runs after the construction try/catch and after
state is published as ready. If that subscription throws, `start()` rejects but
the supervisor remains falsely ready with a live client/transport. This reopens
the exact partial-subscription failure class fixed in 010R. A transport already
terminal before registration can also synchronously notify during start; the
current start then resolves even though the callback changed state to crashed.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Include terminal subscription in the guarded startup transaction.
- A subscription throw closes the partial transport at most once, clears all
  references, leaves state crashed/no client, and makes start reject.
- If late registration synchronously reports that the transport is already
  terminal, start must not report ready; choose/document a rejected-start
  contract and retain the original terminal reason.
- A later start uses a fresh transport and replays nothing.

FINDING 2 — TERMINATION CAN STRAND AN IN-FLIGHT ASYNC WRITE
`send()` awaits `sink.write(bytes)` before it creates a drain waiter. If write
returns a promise that is still pending when EOF/error/close occurs, failClosed
has no waiter to reject. The send promise remains pending indefinitely unless
the underlying write later settles. Terminal means every outstanding send must
settle, including one awaiting the write result rather than drain.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Track/race every in-flight send phase against one terminal signal so EOF,
  read/write error or explicit close promptly rejects writes awaiting either
  write acceptance or drain.
- Cleanup listeners/waiters after settlement; repeated terminal events cannot
  settle or notify twice.
- Retain the actual terminal cause where available, including for a terminal
  observer registered after termination.

FINDING 3 — BACKPRESSURE AND LINE-LIMIT CONTRACTS HAVE EDGE GAPS
Concurrent sends continue calling sink.write after a prior write returned false;
the transport is therefore not actually respecting the sink's backpressure.
Also, inbound maxLineBytes excludes LF in the exact-boundary test but temporarily
counts CR, so an exact-limit payload accepted with LF is rejected with CRLF.
Outbound counts its LF in the cap, making the same named limit inconsistent in
the two directions.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Serialize outbound sends. Once a write returns false, do not invoke the next
  write until drain. Preserve invocation order. A terminal event rejects the
  queued and active sends; no queued write occurs afterward.
- Define maxLineBytes consistently as UTF-8 JSON PAYLOAD bytes, excluding the
  LF or CRLF delimiter, inbound and outbound. Exact-limit LF and CRLF payloads
  pass; payload limit+1 fails. A CR may exceed the payload cap only while it is
  the possible CRLF delimiter; if the following byte is not LF, fail closed.
- Validate configured limits as positive safe integers before subscribing to
  endpoints; invalid configuration throws without a half-created transport.
- State coherently whether explicit close notifies `onTerminal`. Preferred:
  every terminal, including explicit close, notifies once; supervisor clean
  stop remains stopped because it enters stopping before calling close. Make
  comments, implementation and tests agree.

FINDING 2 CORRECTED — IN-FLIGHT WRITE RACE
jsonLineTransport.ts: every send phase races against ONE shared terminal
observer set. The write-acceptance phase registers an observer that is
removed on settlement; a never-settling async sink.write is promptly
rejected by EOF, read error, write error AND explicit close (bounded
200 ms test race — a regression fails instead of hanging). Drain waiters

  no further write runs until drain; invocation order preserved
  (regression: second send's write is not invoked before drain, then
  both proceed in order). Terminal while one send is active and another
  queued rejects BOTH with the cause, performs no later write, and
  closes/notifies once.

  LF/CRLF delimiter, inbound AND outbound. A trailing CR is treated as
  the possible CRLF delimiter (not counted); if the following byte is
  not LF, or a second CR arrives, the CR becomes payload and the cap is
  re-checked fail-closed. Regressions: exact payload cap passes with
  both LF and CRLF; payload cap+1 fails; CR-then-non-LF counts toward
  the cap; outbound exact payload succeeds though the frame is one byte
  larger. The receive-buffer cap counts raw bytes and fires while the
  content stays under the line cap (CR-heavy unterminated stream).
- Limits are validated as positive safe integers (and buffer >= line)
  BEFORE any endpoint subscription; invalid config throws without a
  half-created transport (regression: zero/negative/fractional/NaN/
  infinite and buffer<line all throw; no endpoint end() ever runs).
- Coherent close policy: EVERY terminal, including explicit close,
  notifies observers once; the supervisor's clean stop stays stopped
  because it enters `stopping` before calling close(). Comments in
  windowCapabilityTypes.ts and the supervisor now state this, matching
  implementation and tests.

GOAL — NEXT SMALL PROOF
Add the Papers-side ownership adapter that can wrap ONE injected child-like
helper process and expose the already accepted WindowTransport. Tests use only
fake child/stdin/stdout/stderr objects. This proves process lifecycle and stream
adaptation before choosing or launching a real packaged helper.

WHY THIS LAYER NOW
009 proved the native verbs; 010 proved the six-command client/supervisor; 011
proved bounded JSON framing. The missing seam is safe ownership of a helper
process. Keep launch choice separate: PowerShell 7 availability on this machine
is evidence, not yet an accepted packaging dependency.

ALLOWED SCOPE
- Prefer one new adapter under src/main/windows/ and one focused test file.
- Minimally extend the 010/011 types/transport only if a fake-process lifecycle
  cannot otherwise be expressed; justify every extension.
- Accept an injected zero-argument `createProcess()` factory returning a narrow
  child-like interface. Do not accept executable paths, scripts, arguments,
  command strings, environment maps or working directories through the public
  adapter API. Launch policy belongs to a later main-process composition root.
- Adapt child stdout chunks/EOF/error to TransportSource; child stdin write/
  drain/error/end to TransportSink; pass these into createJsonLineTransport.

REQUIRED OWNERSHIP CONTRACT
1. One adapter owns exactly one process instance. It exposes WindowTransport,
   not the raw child or raw stream endpoints.
2. Child factory throw, missing required stdio, or stream subscription throw
   fails construction closed, best-effort terminates any partial child once,
   and exposes no transport.
3. stdout EOF/error, stdin error, child `error`, or child `exit` are one shared
   terminal event with the first cause retained. Duplicate/reordered terminal
   signals notify once and close/terminate endpoints/process at most once.
4. Explicit transport close ends stdin, detaches/ends readable ownership as the
   narrow fake contract permits, then requests graceful child termination once.
   It is idempotent and retains the coherent 011 terminal notification policy.
5. Backpressure crosses the adapter faithfully: a false stdin write plus drain
   behaves exactly as 011; async/sync write failure is terminal.
6. Bound stderr diagnostics by encoded bytes with a small configurable cap.
   Preserve only a truncated diagnostic for lifecycle error text; stderr is
   never parsed as protocol, never delivered to onMessage, and cannot grow
   without bound.
7. No implicit restart and no request replay. The existing supervisor alone may
   call its injected factory again after crash; a fresh start owns a new fake
   process and sends nothing.
8. No raw send/exec/spawn/shell/eval surface reaches product callers. The
   capability client remains the only command vocabulary.

MANDATORY REGRESSIONS
- fragmented/coalesced fake stdout reaches the existing framing/schema gate;
- stdin false waits for drain; sync/async stdin failure terminates once;
- stdout end/error, stdin error, child error/exit in varied orders collapse to
  one preserved terminal cause and one child termination;
- close twice ends/terminates once; events after close do nothing;
- factory throw, missing stdio and subscription throw clean partial ownership;
- stderr below/exact/over cap uses encoded bytes, truncates deterministically,
  and never becomes a protocol message;
- supervisor crash rejects pending once; same-supervisor restart creates a
  second fake process, sends/replays nothing, and the first process is not
  reused;
- exported product-facing surfaces contain no executable/script/args/env/cwd or
  raw process/stream access.

OUT OF SCOPE / FORBIDDEN

executable discovery, native window, socket/pipe, dependency, product
hostFacade/IPC/preload/renderer/As you Go wiring, persistence, UI, build,

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 011R REVIEW: NARROW CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
The three rejected mechanisms are substantially corrected. BRAIN inspected the
implementation and reran 44/44 focused tests, the full 22-file / 201-test suite,
typecheck and diff check successfully. Do not commit Papers yet: one real limit
edge remains, and two mandatory regressions do not exercise what their names
claim. This is a narrow evidence/correctness pass, not another redesign.

FINDING 1 — CONSECUTIVE CR PAYLOAD DOES NOT RECHECK maxLineBytes IMMEDIATELY
In the `byte === CR` branch, a second CR proves the previous CR was payload and
increments `contentBytes`, but there is no line-cap check in that branch. A long
CR-only unterminated line may therefore exceed maxLineBytes and continue until
the larger receive-buffer cap. This contradicts the checkpoint statement that
the cap is rechecked when a second CR arrives.

FINDING 2 — NEVER-SETTLING WRITE TESTS MAY TERMINATE BEFORE write() STARTS
`send()` begins through a promise-chain microtask. The test calls finish()/close()
immediately after send(), without waiting for the fake sink to report that its
never-settling write was invoked. It can therefore pass by rejecting a queued
send before `sink.write` starts, leaving the original in-flight-write race
unproven even though the implementation appears designed to handle it.

FINDING 3 — FRESH RETRY TESTS CREATE A DIFFERENT SUPERVISOR
Both new startup-failure regressions construct a separate `retry` supervisor.
The requirement is that the SAME crashed supervisor can call start() again,
obtain a fresh transport/client from its factory, and replay nothing. Existing
010 coverage does not prove the two new terminal-subscription paths reset all
their local state correctly.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 012 REVIEW: CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
Do not commit Papers yet. The adapter is isolated and its ordinary lifecycle,
backpressure, terminal arbitration and same-supervisor restart are sound in the
covered cases. BRAIN reran all 58 focused tests, the full 23-file / 215-test
suite, typecheck and diff check successfully. Three ownership requirements are
missing despite the green suite.

FINDING 1 — OUT-OF-BAND STDIN ERROR CANNOT BE OBSERVED
The assignment explicitly requires a stdin error to enter the shared terminal
path. `ChildLikeProcess.stdin` is only TransportSink, which has write/onDrain/end
but no error subscription. The adapter covers thrown/rejected writes, not a
writable stream emitting an error independently after a successful write.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Give only the child-side stdin seam a narrow error subscription; do not widen
  the accepted generic JSON TransportSink unless necessary.
- Subscribe during guarded construction and feed it into the same first-cause
  terminal callback as stdout/child events.
- A stdin error before, during or after a pending/backpressured write terminates
  once, rejects the send/pending request once, and later exit/error/EOF signals
  cannot replace its cause.

FINDING 2 — STDERR OWNERSHIP IS NEVER ENDED
stderr is subscribed for chunks but its `end()` is never called on explicit
close or any other terminal path. Its callback also continues collecting data
after termination (bounded, but still owned). The contract says readable
ownership is ended/detached; that includes diagnostic stderr, not only stdout.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Include stderr in the adapter's owned readable shutdown. End stdout and
  stderr at most once each on every terminal/explicit-close/construction-cleanup
  path the narrow seam permits.
- Ignore stderr chunks after terminal. Events after close must change neither
  diagnostics nor terminal state.
- Endpoint end failures remain best-effort and cannot cause duplicate child
  termination or replace the first terminal cause.

FINDING 3 — INVALID STDERR CAP CAN REMOVE THE BOUND
maxStderrBytes is not validated. With NaN, `stderrByteCount >= maxStderrBytes`
is always false, allowing unbounded accumulation. Infinity has the same effect.
The claimed encoded-byte bound therefore is not fail-closed for runtime input.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Validate maxStderrBytes before calling createProcess or subscribing anywhere.
  Choose/document non-negative safe integer (allowing 0 to disable diagnostic
  retention) or positive safe integer; reject NaN, infinities, fractions and
  negatives synchronously.
- Invalid configuration must not invoke createProcess, subscribe, end or kill.
- At cap 0, if allowed, retain no bytes but mark deterministic truncation when
  input arrives.

TEST-SURFACE FINDING
The exported-surface regression stringifies one function and searches a few
words. That does not establish module exports or return-object keys. Retain it
if useful, but add direct assertions that the returned object has only the
WindowTransport keys and exposes no child/stdin/stdout/stderr/kill handle. At
runtime module exports must contain no spawn/exec/fork function.

VERDICT
012R corrects the independent stdin-error path, complete ownership shutdown,

tests, the full 23-file / 221-test suite, typecheck and diff check successfully.
Do not commit Papers yet: one construction guard is materially incomplete, and
one regression again terminates before proving the phase named by the test.

FINDING 1 — `isChildLike` DOES NOT VALIDATE REQUIRED STREAM METHODS
The guard requires stdin.onError and truthy stdout/stderr, but does not validate
stdin write/onDrain/end or stdout/stderr onChunk/onEnd/end. A child with missing
methods can pass construction and expose a transport, failing only on a later
send/close. “Missing required stdio fails construction closed” must cover the
required callable shape, not merely object presence.

FINDING 2 — “ACTIVE BACKPRESSURED” STDIN-ERROR TEST IS STILL PRE-WRITE
The test calls send() and immediately emits the independent error. As in 011,
send begins on a promise-chain microtask, so sink.write may never be called. The
test can pass by rejecting a queued send and does not prove that a stdin error
interrupts a write that returned false and is waiting for drain.

EVIDENCE GAP — ASYNC END REJECTION
The checkpoint claims endpoint end rejections are covered, but the new test only
throws synchronously from end(). The implementation catches promise rejection;
add the direct regression so this lifecycle claim is executable evidence.

  requestId/method correlation; sequential, COALESCED (two lines in one
  write -> in-order responses), and FRAGMENTED (request split mid-write
  at byte level) all proven; stdout is protocol JSON only.
- Fail-closed live cases: unknown method -> malformed; command-like
  field (exec) -> denied, never executed; malformed JSON -> typed
  malformed; dead runtime id -> missing, no act.
- EOF shutdown: helper exits cleanly (exit code 0), fresh helper's FIRST
  output is the response to its first request and shows no trace of the
  closed window -> no mutation replay.

SAFETY IDENTITY (each runtime)
- Unique high-entropy title (AYG-013-<pid>-<8hex>); zero-match before
  the window exists -> no act; exactly-one discovery; observed PID and
  title matched the recorded identity before every mutation.
- Lifecycle on the ONE disposable window: observe normal 400x300 ->
  minimize -> restore -> apply x=200 y=180 360x260 (toleranced
  round-trip within 8px) -> close -> observe missing; form disposed.

NARROWEST RUNTIME COMPATIBILITY FACT (the packaging decision)
- The 009 adapter and the new helper run UNCHANGED under Windows
  PowerShell 5.1. Two 5.1 adaptations in window-capability.ps1 only:
  #Requires -Version 7.0 -> 5.1 (adapter contains no PS7-only syntax)
  and the PS7-only literal `0u` -> `[uint32]0`. helper-jsonline.ps1
  normalizes parsed JSON to nested hashtables (no -AsHashtable) and
  uses [double]::IsNaN/IsInfinity (no .NET Core-only IsFinite).

CLEANUP PROOF
- finally-style on every path: window closed via the harness, helpers
  EOF'd then killed via retained handles if needed. Per-run and
  post-matrix verification: 0 owned helper PIDs still running; 0
  AYG-013-* windows remain; an external enumeration after the run also
  reports 0. Transcript: probes/native-window/probe-013-transcript.txt
  (runtimes, discovered identities, cleanup line).

======================================================================
[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 013 REVIEW: CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
Do not commit 013 yet. The authorized native lifecycle genuinely ran on both
PowerShell 7.6.3 and Windows PowerShell 5.1, and BRAIN independently verified
afterward that zero helper-jsonline processes and zero `AYG-013-*` windows
remain. The core compatibility result is valuable. However, the helper wire
format and identity enforcement do not yet satisfy the already committed 010R
contract, so the successful live harness currently proves a parallel protocol.

FINDING 1 — SUCCESS RESPONSES FAIL THE PAPERS SCHEMA GATE
The helper serializes `runtimeId` as a JSON number in list and observation
payloads. `parseWindowResponse` requires a non-empty string. Papers would drop
every successful list/observe/minimize/restore/apply response as malformed.
The fake test passes because PowerShell loosely compares number 4097 to string
"4097"; it does not assert the JSON type.

FINDING 2 — INVALID RESPONSES ALSO CANNOT CORRELATE
The helper claims positive-safe request IDs but accepts up to Int64.MaxValue,
then casts them to `[int]` in ConvertTo-AygResponse. Values above Int32 can crash
the loop; values above JavaScript MAX_SAFE_INTEGER violate 010R. Invalid JSON
uses requestId 0/method `unknown`, and an unknown request method is echoed as
`pwn`; both response envelopes are rejected by the Papers schema gate. A
malformed request without a valid correlatable id/method cannot produce a valid
typed response under the accepted response union.

REQUIRED POLICY
- runtimeId is always a non-empty decimal STRING on the wire, list and every
  observation. Parsing back to IntPtr remains helper-internal.
- requestId is a positive integer <= 9007199254740991, stored/echoed without an
  Int32 narrowing cast.
- Align invalid-request behavior with 011: an unparseable line, invalid id, or
  unknown method is ignored (no stdout response, no act). To prove liveness,
  coalesce each invalid line before a valid request and assert the next/only
  response is the valid request's correlated response. A valid known-method
  request containing forbidden command-like fields may return typed `denied`.
- Add an explicit wire-schema validator to probe tests and assert every emitted
  response matches 010R shapes/types; do not rely on PowerShell coercion.

FINDING 3 — MUTATIONS ARE NOT BOUND TO RECORDED IDENTITY
The harness observes PID/title immediately before mutation, but the helper only
checks `IsWindow(HWND)` inside the mutation request. That is a time-of-check/
time-of-use gap and does not meet the dispatch rule that the helper verify the
recorded PID/title still match before acting. HWND reuse could route a command
to a different window between the harness check and helper mutation.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Maintain a helper-session identity registry populated from list/observe
  results: runtimeId string -> recorded PID + exact title.
- Immediately before minimize/restore/apply/close, in the same request handler,
  observe the live HWND and require both PID and exact title to equal the
  registry entry. Missing registry entry, vanished HWND or mismatch returns a
  typed fail-closed outcome and performs no operation.
- Fake regressions must replace the same runtime id with a different PID/title
  and prove every mutation touches neither old nor replacement. Live harness
  still verifies the selected unique title/PID, but helper enforcement is the
  authoritative final check.
- Keep initial unique-title selection outside the six-command wire vocabulary:
  implement/test a harness resolver that maps list results to typed
  missing/ambiguous/success and never mutates on zero/multiple. Do not add a
  seventh helper command. The live run may prove missing + success; fake data
  proves ambiguous without launching a second window.

FINDING 4 — NATIVE/NUMERIC ERRORS CAN KILL THE HELPER
Bounds accept finite values outside Int32 and dispatch casts them to `[int]`;
native functions throw under `$ErrorActionPreference = Stop`; Invoke-AygRequest
has no operation-level catch. A malformed/denied request can therefore end the
long-lived helper rather than return fail-closed while preserving later request
liveness.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Validate x/y/width/height as platform-representable values before casts;
  choose/document integer or deterministic rounding policy, with positive
  width/height. Reject overflow and unsupported state values. Do not silently
  ignore an `apply.state` field: either implement its accepted semantics or
  return typed malformed/denied for now.
- Catch native observation/mutation exceptions per valid request, return a
  valid typed denied/missing outcome with bounded non-sensitive error text, and
  keep the helper alive. Fake a throwing op, then send a valid request and prove
  correlation/liveness.

FINDING 5 — FRAGMENTATION AND TRANSCRIPT CLAIMS ARE INCOMPLETE
The “fragmented UTF-8” live request contains ASCII only. The saved transcript
omits per-runtime assertion counts, helper exit codes, state/bounds transitions
and the final assertion summary claimed by the checkpoint.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Split a request inside an actual multibyte UTF-8 character (a known-method
  observe with a nonnumeric multibyte target can validly yield malformed), then
  prove the following valid request still correlates.
- Transcript each runtime's runtime/version/path, assertion pass/fail count,
  helper exit codes, normal->minimized->normal transition, applied/observed
  bounds, fresh-helper no-replay result, and cleanup counts. Keep unrelated
  windows/environment out. Write the final summary before saving the transcript.
- Update the adapter header that still says PowerShell 7 is required.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 013R REVIEW: NARROW ARCHITECTURE CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
013R corrects the JSON wire types, invalid-line correlation policy, numeric
hardening, native exception containment, real multibyte fragmentation and
transcript completeness. The fake suite passes 165/165, and cleanup is again
independently confirmed at zero helper processes / zero `AYG-013-*` windows.
Do not commit yet: the current registry can bless HWND reuse, and the helper
still exposes/accepts the raw HWND rather than a true session capability token.

FINDING 1 — OBSERVE/LIST OVERWRITES THE IDENTITY IT IS MEANT TO PROTECT
`Register-AygIdentity` unconditionally replaces the registry entry. After a
same-HWND PID/title change, the fake test deliberately calls observe, overwrites
the old identity, then permits minimize on the replacement. In the product,
continuous observation/list refresh is normal; it must never convert a stale
layout member into authority over a different window. The harness's expected-
identity comparison hides this, but helper-side enforcement must stand alone.

FINDING 2 — “OPAQUE RUNTIME ID” IS THE DECIMAL RAW HWND
The wire id is `[string][long]$hWnd`, and requests parse arbitrary numeric
strings back into IntPtr. That exposes the native handle and permits guessing.
010 intentionally branded RuntimeWindowId as session-only/opaque and separated
it from persisted descriptors. The helper should issue capabilities, not expose
the OS handle namespace.

REQUIRED ARCHITECTURE
- On list, create a high-entropy helper-session token for each newly observed
  `(HWND, PID, exact title)` identity. Store token -> HWND/PID/title. Repeated
  list calls for the unchanged identity return the same token within that
  helper session.
- If an HWND is reused with different PID/title, create a NEW token for the new
  identity while the old token remains bound to the old identity and fails
  closed. Never overwrite/rebind a token.
- observe/minimize/restore/apply/close accept only issued tokens. Unknown,
  guessed, raw numeric HWND or token from a previous helper session => missing,
  no native observation/mutation.
- Before every observe and mutation, resolve the token internally and recheck
  IsWindow + exact PID/title in that same handler. Identity mismatch => denied;
  vanished => missing. Observe must not re-register or repair a mismatched token.
- No raw HWND appears in stdout, transcript or product-facing error text.
- A fresh helper issues fresh tokens and replays nothing; persisted descriptors
  remain unable to contain a token.

MANDATORY TOKEN REGRESSIONS
- unchanged identity gets stable token across repeated list;
- two identities get distinct nonempty, nonnumeric high-entropy tokens;
- raw numeric HWND and random/previous-session token are missing/no act;
- same HWND replacement receives a new token on list; old token remains denied
  across repeated list/observe and every mutation; new token can act only after
  the resolver selects it;
- helper restart changes tokens and old token is unusable;
- live transcript records tokens only (never HWND) and confirms helper-side
  PID/title checks before the disposable window mutations.

FINDING 3 — SCHEMA VALIDATOR AND CROSS-RUNTIME CLAIM NEED FINISHING
The validator checks only part of WindowObservation/payload shapes. It does not
fully assert title/processId/processPath/state/bounds types, exact success
payload by method, or absence of payload on non-success/close. Also the test
declares PowerShell 5.1 compatibility but uses PS7-only `ConvertFrom-Json
-AsHashtable`; it was run only under pwsh. The checkpoint says MAX_SAFE was
proven live, but run-013 never sends it and Send-AygRequest narrows RequestId to
`[int]`.

REQUIRED EVIDENCE
- Complete the probe validator to mirror all 010R response predicates and
  method/outcome payload rules without coercive comparisons.
- Make helper-013.test.ps1 actually run unchanged under both PowerShell 5.1 and
  7 (normalize JSON without `-AsHashtable`) and report both counts.
- Change the harness request id parameter to `[long]`; live-send
  9007199254740991 on each runtime and assert exact correlated echo. Keep
  MAX_SAFE+1 ignored-before-valid proof.
- Lower window-capability.test.ps1's stale #Requires only if it contains no PS7
  dependency, then run its 11 tests under both runtimes; otherwise document the
  precise blocker rather than claiming cross-runtime adapter tests.
- Rerun the authorized live matrix on both runtimes, update per-runtime/final
  transcript counts before save, and independently verify cleanup again.

======================================================================
[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 013R2 REVIEW: FINAL BOUNDEDNESS CORRECTION
======================================================================

VERDICT
013R2's token behavior is correct and independently passes 437/437 under both
PowerShell runtimes; the 009 adapter passes 11/11 under both, diff-check is
clean, and cleanup is independently zero helpers / zero `AYG-013-*` windows.
Do not commit yet. Two narrow architecture defects remain in this prospective
long-lived helper, plus the claimed 010R test mirror is still weaker than 010R.

FINDING 1 - OBSOLETE RAW-HWND CONTRACT REMAINS IN THE SOURCE
The file header still says runtimeId is a decimal string, and the unused
`Get-AygWireRuntimeId` still converts an HWND to that wire form. Even dead code
is the wrong future integration seam here. Correct the header and remove the
function; token issuance must be the only product-facing identity path.

FINDING 2 - SESSION TOKEN STORAGE IS UNBOUNDED
`byToken` and `byKey` only grow. Repeated window churn would grow a helper that
is intended to live for the Papers session. Bound the registry explicitly:
- use a documented fixed probe limit of 4096 issued tokens per helper session;
- before a list issues any new token, preflight the full list atomically;
- if existing count + distinct new identities would exceed the limit, return a
  typed `denied` envelope with bounded non-sensitive text, issue NO new tokens,
  return NO partial windows payload, and leave both maps unchanged;
- do not evict/rebind old tokens. Thus an old HWND-reuse token retains the
  already-proven denied semantics, and known tokens remain usable at capacity;
- no HWND may appear in capacity errors or other product-facing text.
Use a small injected/overridden test limit to prove: fill succeeds; the next
new identity makes list denied atomically; map counts do not change; previously
issued tokens still resolve; restarting/resetting the helper resets capacity.

FINDING 3 - THE WIRE VALIDATOR STILL COERCES AND OMITS 010R PREDICATES
`[string]$Obs.runtimeId` and `[string]$Obs.state` accept coerced values.
processId does not enforce null-or-nonnegative-safe-integer. Bounds do not
enforce null-or-finite values with positive width/height. `missing` is absent
from the state union, and an optional error is not type-checked. Make the probe
validator faithfully mirror committed 010R semantics without coercion:
- runtimeId is a nonempty string and, for this helper, token-shaped;
- title string; processId null or nonnegative safe integer; processPath null or
  string; state is normal/minimized/maximized/missing;
- bounds is null or a mapping with finite numeric x/y/width/height and strictly
  positive width/height;
- requestId is a positive safe integer; optional error is a string;
- retain strict method/outcome payload rules already present.
Add direct negative validator fixtures so numeric runtimeId, fractional or
negative processId, invalid state, NaN/infinite/non-positive bounds, bad error,
and wrong payload shapes are demonstrably rejected rather than merely absent
from helper output. A pure boolean predicate plus assertions is acceptable.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 013R3 REVIEW: STRUCTURAL SCHEMA CORRECTION
======================================================================

VERDICT
The helper architecture is now acceptable: the obsolete raw-HWND seam is
removed and the 4096-token capacity guard is atomic, bounded and fail-closed.
Independent execution confirms 193/193 fake tests and 11/11 adapter tests under
both runtimes, diff-check clean, and cleanup 0/0. Do not commit yet because the
test validator still accepts several wire shapes that committed 010R rejects.

FINAL FINDING - REQUIRED/ARRAY/OPTIONAL STRUCTURE IS NOT YET MIRRORED
- A successful list checks only that `windows` is present; it does not require
  an actual array. A scalar observation mapping can therefore pass as windows.
- `processId`, `processPath`, and `bounds` are required observation keys whose
  values may be null. Indexing an absent key also yields null in PowerShell, so
  the current predicate incorrectly accepts those keys when omitted.
- `error` is optional, but when present it must be a string. The current test
  treats an explicitly present null exactly like an absent key and accepts it.
- `ConvertTo-PsHashtable` must preserve JSON arrays even when empty or carrying
  one member; otherwise an honest array can be unrolled before validation.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 014 REVIEW: CORRECTION REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
The Papers-owned scripts retain 013 behavior (205/205 under both runtimes),
focused TS tests pass 25/25, full unit tests pass 248/248, typecheck is clean,
no helper remains, and the pre-existing dirty host/runtime files were not added
to 014 scope. Do not commit yet: the production ownership/validation layer does
not enforce several guarantees claimed by the assignment and checkpoint.

FINDING 1 - THE "PROVENANCE LOCK" DOES NOT LOCK CONTENT
Validation accepts any nonempty protocolVersion, any nonempty file list and any
nonempty file contents. A one-byte script edit passes; changing the manifest
and listed paths can also pass; path separators/traversal are not rejected.
The current "tampered" tests cover only missing/empty files. This does not stop
the external resource from silently drifting away from the reviewed app code.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Define the exact accepted protocol version and exact two resource basenames.
  Reject wrong version, duplicates, extras, separators/traversal and any
  mismatch between the supplied paths and canonical files under the directory.
- Pin SHA-256 for both resource files in main-process code and manifest. Runtime
  validation must require manifest hashes == compiled expected hashes AND
  actual bytes == expected hashes. A deliberate future helper edit therefore
  requires an explicit reviewed code+manifest update; resource-only tampering
  fails before spawn.
- Validate manifest executable/runtime identity and fixed argument prefix
  against the compiled spawn contract rather than merely documenting them.
- Add negative tests for one-byte script mutation, manifest-only hash/version/
  file-list/argument tampering, traversal/extra/duplicate entries and mismatched
  path objects. Keep errors bounded and do not disclose script contents.

FINDING 2 - `powershell.exe` IS SEARCHED, NOT A VALIDATED SYSTEM RUNTIME
Spawning the bare name depends on Windows search/PATH/current-directory rules,
and the factory never proves the runtime exists before attempting child
creation. The assignment explicitly required runtime availability validation
before spawn. A same-named executable beside/writable ahead of Papers must not
be eligible.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Resolve an absolute Windows PowerShell 5.1 executable beneath the trusted
  Windows directory (System32/WindowsPowerShell/v1.0/powershell.exe) from
  main-process-owned environment/platform input; reject missing, non-absolute
  or unexpected paths before child creation. Never use PATH lookup.
- The spawn spec's `file` is that validated absolute path; arguments/options
  remain fixed, shell false, hidden and pipe-only. No renderer input.
- Unit-test absolute runtime resolution, missing runtime pre-spawn, and that no
  bare executable/PATH/cwd/env override enters the spawn spec.

FINDING 3 - VALIDATED PATH MAY NOT BE THE SPAWNED PATH
`start()` validates `resolvePaths()`, but the transport closure calls
`resolvePaths()` again when creating the child. A changing resolver can make a
valid first result authorize a different unvalidated helper path. Repeated and
concurrent starts also redo resolution even when no new child is needed.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- One start attempt resolves the runtime and resource paths exactly once,
  validates that immutable snapshot, and creates the child from the SAME
  snapshot. Never call either resolver again inside the transport closure.
- Coalesce concurrent starts; ready/idempotent start performs no second resolve,
  validation or spawn. A post-crash restart performs one fresh validation and
  creates one fresh child with no replay.
- Resolver/stat/hash/read exceptions become typed helper-unavailable and zero
  child attempts. Add alternating-resolver and throwing-resolver regressions,
  plus counters proving invalid resources/runtime invoke createProcess zero
  times (the current malformed-manifest test counts a different harness and is
  not proof).

FINDING 4 - PACKAGE INCLUSION IS UNTESTED AND COUNTS ARE MISREPORTED
There is no test reading electron-builder.yml, despite the mandatory package-
configuration gate. The checkpoint's distribution is also inaccurate: current
files contain 8 resource tests, 5 spawn tests and 12 factory tests (25 total),
not 7/6/12.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Add a dependency-free test that asserts exactly one intended
  `resources/window-helper -> window-helper` extraResources mapping and no
  broader resources/tests mapping for this helper.
- Report exact new per-file and total counts from the actual runner.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 014R REVIEW: TWO FINAL CORRECTIONS
======================================================================

VERDICT
014R now pins resource bytes/version/manifest contract, resolves an absolute
system PowerShell runtime, uses one validated start snapshot and tests the
builder mapping. Do not commit yet: one resource predicate and one lifecycle
race still violate explicit 014 gates, while their named tests do not actually
exercise the claimed cases.

FINDING 1 - DUPLICATE FILE LIST IS ACCEPTED
`parseResourceFileList` checks length and membership but not uniqueness or that
every expected basename occurs. `[window-helper.ps1, window-helper.ps1]` passes
the parser. The current duplicate test writes fake bytes, so validation later
fails on the helper hash and the test passes for the wrong reason. With the real
helper bytes and compiled manifest hashes, that duplicate list can skip all
existence/content validation of `window-capability.ps1`.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Require set size == 2 and every canonical basename exactly once (order may be
  irrelevant). Return the file-list reason before any file hash/stat work.
- Build the duplicate fixture with real accepted helper bytes and a missing or
  mutated adapter, assert rejection specifically at the file-list gate, and add
  the symmetric adapter/adapter duplicate.

FINDING 2 - CONCURRENT STOP CAN LET START REPORT READY
The original 014 mandatory matrix included concurrent start/stop, but no such
test exists. `start()` awaits an already-constructed supervisor while `stop()`
can transition it to stopping/stopped; the start continuation then returns
`ready` unconditionally. A start overtaken by stop must not report ready.

REQUIRED CORRECTION
- Define deterministic factory semantics: stop invalidates any in-flight start;
  the overtaken start resolves `helper-unavailable`, final state is stopped,
  pending work is rejected once and the one created child is owned/terminated.
- After both settle, a new start performs one fresh resolve/validation/spawn and
  can become ready. Repeated/concurrent stop remains idempotent.
- Add a controllable fake transport/child regression that genuinely overlaps
  start and stop rather than executing them sequentially.

FINDING 3 - THE "ALTERNATING RESOLVER" FIXTURE DOES NOT ALTERNATE
That test uses the ordinary stable harness resolver. A call-count of one is
useful but does not prove a second result would be unsafe. Replace or extend it
with a resolver returning the accepted directory first and throwing or
returning a distinct invalid path on any later call; assert the later branch is
never reached and the captured spawn snapshot is exactly the first object.

[2026-08-06 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 014R2 REVIEW: FINAL LIFECYCLE GAP
======================================================================

VERDICT
R2 correctly rejects the two-entry symmetric duplicates, makes stop-over-start
report unavailable, and proves one immutable resolver result. Do not commit
yet: the exact-list predicate still has a mixed-duplicate hole, and the reverse
start/stop ordering can create an unowned child.

FINDING 1 - LIST LENGTH IS NOT ENFORCED
The parser requires `Set.size === 2` and both expected names, but no longer
requires `list.length === 2`. `[helper, adapter, helper]` therefore passes even
though the contract says every basename exactly once. Require both array length
and set size to equal the exact expected length. Add mixed-duplicate fixtures in
both orders and retain reversed unique-order acceptance if order is irrelevant.

FINDING 2 - START-DURING-STOP / CONCURRENT-STOP CAN STRAND A CHILD
R2 tests `start(); stop()`. It does not test a ready helper followed by
`stop(); start()` while transport close is pending. The supervisor's stop sets
`stopping`, clears/captures the old transport and awaits close. A new start is
currently allowed from `stopping`, creates a new child, and the old stop later
sets state to stopped without closing that new transport. Concurrent stop calls
can similarly let one stop report stopped before the other's close finishes,
allowing a premature restart and the same leak.

REQUIRED SEMANTICS
- Factory stop calls coalesce onto one in-flight stop promise; one transport is
  closed/killed once and state is not considered restartable until it settles.
- `start()` while stop is in flight returns helper-unavailable with ZERO new
  resolve/validation/spawn. The caller may retry after stop settles; that retry
  performs exactly one fresh snapshot/spawn and becomes ready.
- Preserve R2 stop-over-start behavior and ordinary idempotent start/stop.
- Use a controllable close gate in tests so stop genuinely remains pending.
  Prove: ready -> pending stop -> start rejected/no spawn -> concurrent second
  stop shares teardown -> release close -> stopped -> fresh start ready; final
  cleanup owns both generations exactly once with no unreachable child.

[2026-08-07 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 015R CORRECTION + DISPATCH
MAKE THE ONE-WINDOW PROOF A SAFE, DURABLE FOUNDATION
======================================================================

REVIEW OUTCOME
015 is a valuable live proof, but is NOT accepted or commit-ready yet. Keep
every working behavior and the isolated live transcript; correct the contract
gaps below without beginning multi-window Assignment 016.

The discovered 0x0 desktop rectangle is a real product finding. Keep the
wire-level `bounds:null` fix, add a direct regression for it, and exclude such
non-onscreen observations from the picker instead of allowing one malformed
desktop entry to discard the complete list.

CORRECTION 1 - PERSIST A DURABLE MATCH, NOT A LIVE PID
`{version:1,title,processId}` is not a stable descriptor. It survives only
while that exact process survives and contradicts the earlier rule that live
observations remain runtime state. Replace it in Papers, As you Go, fixtures
and tests with exactly:

  { version: 1, executableFingerprint: <sha256>, title: <bounded exact title> }

- Compute the fingerprint in trusted Papers main from a normalized helper-
  observed executable path. Persist only the fingerprint and title: never the
  raw path, PID, HWND, helper token or binding id.
- Re-resolution enumerates already-visible eligible windows, recomputes the
  fingerprint host-side, matches fingerprint + exact title, and remains
  fail-closed: zero = missing, more than one = ambiguous. A descriptor never
  launches anything.
- Candidate payloads sent to Backpack must not expose processPath or processId.
  Send only opaque candidate id, bounded title, bounded application label,
  bounded icon, and static live state needed by the picker.
- The reload proof must be strengthened: keep the disposable target alive,
  fully stop/restart the owned Papers/helper session, then prove the saved
  descriptor resolves to that target despite a fresh helper and binding. Do
  not call same-process renderer reload alone a durable-identity proof.

CORRECTION 2 - MAIN-OWNED ISSUED BINDINGS
Do not expose the helper runtime token as the Backpack capability. Papers main
must mint a bounded opaque binding id and retain the binding-id -> helper-token
mapping privately. The project receives only `{version:1,bindingId}`; every
observe/minimize/restore/apply call must look up a currently issued binding and
fail closed otherwise. Bound the registry and its eviction/cleanup policy;
stop clears it. Persisted state must contain neither binding ids nor helper
tokens.

CORRECTION 3 - HONEST, EXACT, BYTE-BOUNDED GATES
The checkpoint says unknown fields are rejected, but the current capability,
descriptor and bounds parsers accept extras. Correct BOTH dedicated IPC and
Backpack preload gates:
- exact allowed keys at every nested level, including list's empty payload;
- UTF-8 byte limits, not JavaScript character-count approximations;
- finite/ranged bounds and exact versioned capability/descriptor shapes;
- regression tests that inject an extra key into capability, descriptor,
  bounds and list payload and prove rejection before service invocation.
Bound trusted helper path/title input before hashing/label/icon work. Bound the
icon data URL by bytes as well as cache count, and bound/deduplicate icon work.

CORRECTION 4 - SIMPLE OBSERVATION SEAM, NO DEAD SUBSCRIPTION API
For this one-member proof, explicitly supersede 015's host-subscription wording:
- remove the unused and incorrect `subscribeMember` timer API;
- keep exactly one renderer pull timer for the sole active layout;
- never overlap polls: skip/coalesce while one observation is pending;
- Papers deduplicates concurrent observation for the same issued binding and
  caps total distinct in-flight observation work;
- missing/invalid binding stops recording, drops the cached capability and is
  shown visibly; a later Activate performs fresh fail-closed resolution;
- switching, unlinking, binning/deleting or teardown clears timer/pending flags.
Do not invent push IPC or multi-member scheduling in 015R. Assignment 016 may
extend this proven seam.

CORRECTION 5 - MEMBER/PICKER UX CORRECTNESS
- A successful member minimize/restore click must update the persisted member
  state and queue the coalesced save even when that layout is not recording;
  changing only CSS is insufficient. Observe current live state before deciding
  the toggle, so stale saved intent does not select the wrong action.
- Opening a populated layout's picker must always offer its persisted sole
  member as an actionable unlink row, even when that window is currently
  missing. Other candidates remain inert in the one-member slice. Unlink is
  data-only and never closes the window.
- Keep Activate's bounds + state behavior and echo suppression, but make
  suppression operation/member-scoped rather than one global blind boolean.
  The first genuine creator move after Activate must not be discarded.
- Extend renderer content signatures/patching as needed so missing, icon and
  state changes update only the member interior and never rebuild/reheat graph
  physics.

CORRECTION 6 - BOUNDED BRIDGE AND AWAITED OWNED SHUTDOWN
- `host-bridge.js` currently has an unbounded, timeout-free pending Map. Add a
  finite pending cap and per-request timeout/cleanup (with fake-timer tests).
  Keep only named window methods; no arbitrary request escape hatch.
- `app.on('will-quit', () => void service.stop())` does not await helper
  teardown. Replace it with a guarded, idempotent quit barrier that prevents
  the first owned quit, awaits the bounded service stop, then resumes quitting
  exactly once. Preserve all pre-015 dirty `index.ts` hunks byte-for-byte.
  Prove normal quit, repeated quit signal, stop failure and no-start cases; do
  one isolated live exit check with zero owned helper processes.

TARGETED REGRESSIONS
Papers tests must additionally prove: descriptor survives changed PID; raw path
and PID absent from project candidate/descriptor payloads; runtime token hidden;
unissued/evicted binding denied; exact unknown-key rejection at both gates;
UTF-8 byte boundaries; 0x0 helper observation becomes bounds:null and is not a
candidate; oversized icon/path/title bounded; observation single-flight/cap;
registry/observer cleanup; awaited idempotent quit.

As you Go tests must additionally prove: no PID/path/binding/token persists;
bridge pending cap/timeout cleanup; no overlapping poll; missing stops and later
Activate re-resolves; inactive member click persists state; missing member can
always unlink; activation echo suppression does not swallow the next real move;
member-only patches do not reheat/rebuild the graph.

AUTHORITY / REPOSITORY BOUNDARY
This is a correction within the creator-authorized 015 slice. The existing
authority to build canonical Papers and control ONE uniquely titled disposable
test window in an isolated Papers/userData/project session remains valid. Do
not touch creator windows/data, package/install, update the Start Menu target,
or start 016. Preserve the recorded pre-015 Papers dirty set and stage nothing.

VERIFY AND STOP
Run focused 010-015R, both PowerShell schema runtimes, full Papers unit suite,
typecheck/build; run focused model/bridge/renderer/pointer tests and full As you
Go suite, classifying only the already-recorded three unrelated failures. Run

Papers/helper restart while the disposable target remains alive and final zero
owned-process/window cleanup. Append `WORKER - Assignment 015R CHECKPOINT` with

Do not commit/push or begin 016.

   hit this race; recorded as future-proofing for multi-window slices.

   the preload task map (state-load -> {state}, pick-target ->

   captureWorkspaceView -> updateWorkspaceView merge); the debounced

   normal/minimized/missing).

   the one-shot observe at constant 500ms cadence with its own dedupe

[2026-08-10 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 016 DISPATCH
MULTI-WINDOW CONTROLS WITH CREATOR EYE-CHECK CORRECTION
======================================================================

GATE AND CREATOR CORRECTION
015R3 is accepted and committed in both repositories. The creator completed
the one-member eye check and corrected BRAIN's invented UI: no dedicated
Activate button and no prominent Recording furniture. Those controls are
unnecessary, do not work reliably and the apply path can move windows
offscreen. Per creator direction, this correction belongs inside 016 rather
than a standalone pass.

FIRST: CLEAN UP THE EYE-CHECK SESSION
Close only the isolated eye-check target/Papers instance/helper recorded in the
latest WORKER checkpoint (target 50852/title AYG-015R3-50852-c8744387; Papers
54960; helper 34668). Use normal close/owned cleanup, verify all three are gone,
and do not touch the creator's real Papers processes or data.

CREATOR-REQUIRED INTERACTION MODEL
- Remove the dedicated Activate control and every affirmative `Recording`
  status presentation. Status space remains available for missing, denied,
  partial or error outcomes. Internal observation may continue, but ordinary
  tracking is implicit rather than a mode the creator must operate.
- Adding a window captures its current valid bounds/state immediately; the
  creator never has to press another button to make the member useful.
- A program-icon member occurrence is contextual to its containing layout.
  When the same real window belongs to several layouts, choosing its icon in
  Layout A selects Layout A's saved arrangement for that member; choosing it in
  Layout B selects Layout B's. Subsequent live movement records only into the
  chosen layout context, leaving the other layout's saved member unchanged.
- Preserve normal click toggle without making context switching destructive:
  an icon click from a different layout context safely restores/applies that
  occurrence and selects its context; a later click in the already-current
  context toggles minimize/restore. Ctrl-click remains inner multiselection.
  Group restore/isolate likewise selects the containing layout context for the
  members it acts on.
- Before ANY saved bounds are applied, clamp/translate them to a currently
  visible monitor work area with minimum usable size. A stale/disconnected-
  monitor rectangle must never move a window offscreen. Keep the creator's
  original per-layout intent unchanged in persistence; clamping is the safe
  runtime application. Add direct negative/oversize/disconnected-monitor/DPI
  regressions and a live offscreen assertion.

ORIGINAL 016 OUTCOMES REMAIN REQUIRED
- Picker toggles any eligible onscreen window into/out of the layout, prevents
  duplicates within one layout, and permits the same window in other layouts.
- Compact program-icon member buttons persist in creator-controlled order.
  Live status patches never move or reheat the outer workspace node.
- Ctrl-click inner member multiselection is separate from workspace selection.
  Group minimize and restore operate on selected members when present,
  otherwise all. Isolate restores selected and minimizes only unselected
  members of this layout; it never touches outside windows.
- Dedicated inner reorder drag changes member order only. Crossing a clear
  outside threshold unlinks data-only from this layout; Escape, cancellation or
  inside drop changes nothing. Unlink/delete never closes or moves a window.
- Use one bounded batch/action layer with per-member typed results, shared
  observation deduplication and deterministic command serialization. Mixed
  success/missing/denied results are visible and never guessed.
- Outer drag, selection, sets, folders, Bin, copy, trails, collision footprint
  and hue behavior remain ordinary workspace behavior. No whole-item
  detachment, arbitrary executable launch or close-members command.

VISUAL SAFETY AND PROOF
Controls affect only the compact layout-item interior/picker. No repeating
opacity/fill/luminance/hue animation or large-area flashing is permitted;
status changes occur only from user action or bounded observation. Run focused
and full relevant suites, typecheck/build, diff/EOF/hash gates, then an isolated
2-3 disposable-window proof covering picker toggle, contextual icon switching,
selected/all actions, isolate, reorder, cancel and drag-out unlink, persistence,
offscreen clamping and zero owned-process/window cleanup.

[2026-08-10 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 016 DIRECT-ONSCREEN PICK AMENDMENT
HOVER, HIGHLIGHT, CLICK TO ADD
======================================================================

CREATOR EXPERIENCE
The candidate list must not be the only way to choose windows. From a layout,
the creator can enter an onscreen picking mode, move the pointer over any
eligible application window, see that exact window outlined on hover, and click
to add it to the layout. The list remains available for minimized, obscured or
keyboard-selected windows.

INTERACTION CONTRACT
- Expose two clear affordances without restoring rejected activation furniture:
  direct onscreen pick (primary spatial action) and candidate list (secondary).
- Entering direct pick changes the cursor and waits for a target. Hovering an
  eligible task-worthy window draws one thin, static outline around its current
  bounds. Moving to another window moves the outline; blank/excluded/system/
  Papers surfaces show no selectable highlight.
- Normal click toggles the hovered window into/out of THIS layout and exits
  picking mode after a typed success/duplicate/remove/failure result. Escape or
  right-click cancels with no change. The target window must not receive the
  selection click, move, resize, minimize, focus or close as a side effect.
- Ctrl may keep picking mode open for rapid multi-window addition if this can be
  implemented without ambiguity; otherwise one click per entry is the accepted
  simple behavior. Keyboard focus and cancellation must always remain reliable.
- Support every attached monitor and DPI. Outline placement tracks the real
  window rectangle and disappears immediately on cancel, selection, target
  disappearance, Papers/project close, helper failure or display change.

AUTHORITY AND ARCHITECTURE
- Hover resolution, eligibility and the highlight surface are Papers-owned.
  The sandboxed Backpack may request begin/cancel and receive a bounded typed
  candidate result; it never receives, supplies or persists HWND, PID, process
  path, native coordinates as authority, raw protocol commands or overlay
  window identity.
- Reuse the 016 task-worthy eligibility filter, stable descriptor/binding flow
  and duplicate rules. A click is authorized only for the exact currently
  highlighted host-issued candidate and fails closed if it changes or vanishes.
- Own one bounded pick session globally. A second request replaces/cancels the
  first deterministically; shutdown/project leave always clears overlay, input
  capture, timers/hooks and issued hover candidate.

VISUAL SAFETY (STANDING RULE 2)
Affected area is only a thin perimeter around one hovered window; the window
interior and desktop receive no fill, opacity, luminance or hue change. The
outline colour is static. Bounds may reposition at a pointer-sampled bounded
rate, but colour/opacity do not animate or pulse. No full-screen tint, dimming,
flashing, repeated glow or large-area overlay is allowed.

TEST AND LIVE EVIDENCE
Add native/main/IPC/preload/renderer tests for eligible hover, excluded/system/
Papers surfaces, overlap/topmost resolution, stale-target click, toggle add/
remove, Escape/right-click cancellation, second-session replacement, multi-
monitor/DPI bounds, display removal, project/app shutdown cleanup and zero raw
identity leakage. In the isolated 016 proof, select at least two disposable
windows by direct hover/click and one through the list; verify the highlight
never changes window state and all overlay/helper/test processes clean up.

Include this amendment in `WORKER - Assignment 016 CHECKPOINT`. Do not create a
generic screen-inspection API, global automation hook or reusable overlay SDK.

[2026-08-10 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 016 DIRECT-PICK COLOUR AMENDMENT
GREEN MEMBERS, BLUE ADD, RED REMOVE
======================================================================

CREATOR-SPECIFIED PICKING LANGUAGE
While direct onscreen picking is active for one specific layout:
- Every currently resolved window already belonging to THAT layout receives a
  thin green perimeter outline, so existing membership is visible spatially.
- Hovering an eligible unselected window paints one low-opacity blue overlay
  over that window: clicking will ADD it to this layout.
- Hovering a window already selected in this layout paints one low-opacity red
  overlay over that window: clicking will REMOVE it from this layout.
- Membership in another layout does not make a window red here; colour is
  always relative to the layout that opened picking mode.
- After a successful toggle, update the green member outlines immediately and
  continue/exit according to the direct-pick interaction contract. Cancel,
  project leave, helper loss or pick completion removes every external cue.

VISUAL DETAILS
- Reuse the workspace's existing semantic success/add/remove colours where
  available; do not introduce drifting hues or a second colour system.
- Green is perimeter-only. Blue/red may cover the hovered window interior, but
  must be translucent enough that the real window remains clearly readable.
  Use one uniform static opacity chosen through live eye check; no gradient,
  glow, blur, pulse or repeated transition.
- The hovered add/remove overlay is visually dominant over that window's green
  member outline so the impending click result cannot be confused. Do not show
  blue and red simultaneously.
- The overlay must match the exact visible window rectangle, respect rounded
  corners where supported and never tint adjacent windows or another monitor.

SAFETY — STANDING RULE 2
Affected area: thin perimeters around the current layout's resolved members,
plus the full interior of ONE hovered window for the blue/red wash. Update
rate: only on pick start, pointer entering a different eligible window,
membership toggle or pick end. Colour and opacity are static between those
events. No time-driven opacity/fill/luminance/hue animation, fading, flashing
or large-area screen dimming is permitted.

TEST/PROOF ADDITIONS
Prove layout-relative membership colouring, green-outline refresh after add/
remove, blue add versus red remove, overlap/topmost changes, no simultaneous
blue/red, no cue leakage after every exit/failure path and no cue on excluded
system/Papers windows. The final 016 creator eye check must show both blue-add
and red-remove states against real disposable windows before visual acceptance.

[2026-08-10 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - Assignment 016 REVIEW: FOLLOW-UP REQUIRED
======================================================================

VERDICT
SUBSTANTIAL IMPLEMENTATION, NOT YET EYE-CHECK READY. The 20/20 isolated proof
supports the multi-window operations, direct hover/click, colour states,
offscreen clamp and cleanup claims. Independent suites pass: Papers typecheck,
build and 309/309 Vitest (31 files); As you Go 722/725 with only the three
recorded unrelated failures. No product commit is authorized yet.

PRODUCTION-PATH GAPS FOUND BY BRAIN
1. INPUT CAPTURE STARTS TOO LATE. Overlays begin click-through and become
   capturing only after an eligible hover sample completes. An immediate click
   after entering pick mode, or a click during an 80ms blank-to-window sample
   gap, can reach/focus the underlying creator window instead of being owned by
   the picker. This contradicts the no-side-effect contract; the live proof
   waited for confirmed hover and did not exercise the race.
2. ESCAPE/RIGHT-CLICK ARE NOT RELIABLE. The production overlays use
   showInactive(), never call their exposed focus(), and stop capturing over
   blank/excluded areas. Therefore their renderer keydown/contextmenu handlers
   cannot reliably receive Escape/right-click there. The unit test invokes the
   injected onCancel callback directly; it does not test production input.
3. DISPLAY LIFECYCLE IS INCOMPLETE. Production subscribes only to
   `display-metrics-changed`, not Electron's `display-added` and
   `display-removed`, although the contract and checkpoint claim display
   removal cancellation.
4. NEW IPC GATES ARE NOT FULLY STRICT. windowPickIpc bounds strings by JS
   character count rather than UTF-8 bytes. The Papers-owned overlay click
   listener accepts extra keys, NaN/Infinity and unbounded coordinates. These
   must fail before reaching session/helper logic, matching 015's exact-key,
   byte-bounded, finite/ranged boundary.
5. FAILED BEGIN RETAINS THE RESULT CALLBACK. begin() stores onResult before
   overlay/display setup; the no-display and catch paths call endSession(),
   which does not clear the callback. This retains sender state after a failed
   start and violates deterministic cleanup.
6. PAPERS EXCLUSION IS TOO NARROW. Service excludes only the current test
   process PID. The earlier eye-check picker visibly listed the creator's other
   running `Papers` process; the task-worthy amendment did not add a structural
   all-Papers exclusion or regression. No Papers window may be selectable.

DISPATCH — Assignment 016R
WORKER: correct only the six gaps while preserving accepted 016 behavior.
- Own pointer input from pick start through every exit path, including the
  first sample and blank/excluded areas. Hover must still resolve THROUGH the
  Papers-owned overlays. Blank left click may cancel or report no eligible
  target, but it must not reach the desktop/window beneath. Make Escape and
  right-click work from blank and eligible areas in a real production overlay;
  do not depend on a mock invoking onCancel.
- Subscribe/unsubscribe display-metrics-changed, display-added and
  display-removed. Any one cancels and destroys every overlay/capture.
- Enforce exact overlay action payloads, finite/ranged integer screen points,
  and UTF-8 byte bounds at every new pick IPC/preload boundary. Delete the
  unused `pick:ready` path or use it for a bounded, cleaned-up ready handshake;
  leave no dead listener/message seam.
- Clear onResult/sender closure on every failed begin without suppressing the
  single typed result for active cancel/pick/failure. Prove repeated failed
  begins do not retain callbacks, overlays, listeners or timers.
- Exclude every Papers application window structurally in list and hover—not
  by the English title. Use trusted process metadata/path/product identity;
  preserve legitimate Electron applications. Test current owned Papers,
  another packaged Papers process and an unrelated Electron app separately.
- Remove the empty ensureStarted conditional while in the touched service; it
  carries no behavior and should not ship as dead source.

EVIDENCE
Add production-adapter tests (not callback-only mocks) for immediate first
click interception, blank-area Escape/right-click, exact/finite overlay IPC,
all three display events, failed-begin callback cleanup and all-Papers
exclusion. Rerun Papers full/typecheck/build, both PS schema runtimes, As you Go
focused/full and diff/hash gates. Repeat the bounded live proof with an
immediate click and blank-area cancellation; verify the underlying disposable
window never activates/changes and zero overlay/helper/test processes remain.

Append `WORKER - Assignment 016R CHECKPOINT` and STOP without commit/push,
package/install, creator-state changes, Start Menu update or 017. On source
acceptance BRAIN will dispatch the final running blue-add/red-remove eye check.

untouched; whether `hover=null` is distinguished from timeout/failure; and
whether cleanup/process ownership is proven. You may spawn subagents to audit
separate transcript sections. Append `GAZELLE - 016R PROOF AUDIT` with a table:
claim | decisive artifact/line | supported/not-supported | minimal missing
probe. Avoid redesign unless a claim lacks evidence. STOP.

ROKETPUNCHA — SOURCE/TEST ADVERSARIAL REVIEWER (READ-ONLY)
Review only the six BRAIN 016R gaps plus the ParentPid sentinel regression.
Trace production code paths rather than intended behavior. Check first-click
capture, blank Escape/right-click, three display events, strict UTF-8/exact/
finite IPC gates, failed-begin closure cleanup, structural all-Papers exclusion

You may spawn subagents, one gap per agent, read-only. Append

   Green rgba(76,175,80,0.9) lw2 = #4caf50 = the workspace's existing --success

   (windowPickSession.ts:379); membership = title+fingerprint match (:251-256);

   ring (green drawn first :652, wash on top :660-669) so the imminent result is
   dominant, per amendment.
   SUPPORTED with two nuances:
   a) STALE GREEN: greenRects resolved once at begin (:400), never re-resolved
      mid-session; a member window that moves while picking stays ringed at its
      old rectangle until session end. Smallest fix: re-resolve green inside the
      sample loop. Eye-check impact negligible unless the creator moves a member
      mid-pick.
   b) HUE FIDELITY: blue/red are Papers-side literals (overlay page cannot read
      As you Go tokens). Red #f44336 is brighter than the workspace --danger

   ever show two rows for one program surface?).

   ConvertTo-WhClampedBounds (WC:350-389): min usable 160x120 clamped to the
   work area; work-area selection by largest intersection; stale/disconnected
   rects translated fully onscreen; apply-time only, persisted intent untouched
   (WH:384-387). Live proof lines 16-18: offscreen saved rect applied onscreen

   SUPPORTED. Nuances (low risk): the "primary wins ties" comment vs code -
   first-enumerated area wins when no intersection and primary-first order is

   clamp + SetWindowPos + overlay canvas all share the same physical-pixel
   space, so units are consistent (no fixture for primary-not-first or
   no-monitors). Eye-check: recipe step 4 (applied windows land on a visible
   monitor).

FINAL VERDICT
016's product intent is implemented faithfully in source: no Activate/Recording,
contextual program-icon members, layout-relative green/blue/red with green
reusing the workspace --success colour, no animation anywhere in the pick or
item surfaces, a filtered vertical list picker, and safe apply-time clamping.

SMALLEST EYE-CHECK ACTION (short, experiential - 2 minutes, disposable windows)
1. Layout with two disposable members -> "Pick onscreen": two thin green rings
   appear; nothing else changes anywhere on screen.
2. Hover a third disposable window: it turns translucent blue. Click: it joins,
   its ring turns green, picking ends.
3. Pick again, hover that same window: red wash over its green ring. Click: it
   leaves, ring disappears.
4. In picking, point at empty desktop: nothing highlights. Click/Escape/right-
   click ends picking and the window beneath is untouched.
5. Open "List": ordinary applications only, vertical rows, long titles
   ellipsized, no horizontal scrollbar, no duplicate surfaces.
6. While picking, look at the screen for 3 seconds: nothing pulses, fades or
   flashes.
Report only what looked wrong - the state language (green ring = member, blue =
will add, red = will remove) is the whole judgment.

STOP.

GAZELLE CHECKPOINT: ACCEPTED AS AN AUDIT, NOT AS PRODUCT ACCEPTANCE.
Gazelle established that the current 016R harness invokes left click through
CDP (`window.pickOverlay.click`), synthesizes `contextmenu`, and dispatches a
synthetic `KeyboardEvent`. Those events prove renderer handling but do NOT
prove that the production overlay owns physical OS input. The alleged
immediate-click test also waits for overlay/hover settlement, so it does not
exercise the race. A target remaining merely `normal` does not prove it was
not focused, moved or otherwise affected.

NING CHECKPOINT: ACCEPTED AS PRODUCT-INTENT AUDIT.
Ning found two concrete source gaps beyond the prior six:
7. SAME-TASK SURFACE DEDUP IS ABSENT. Structural exclusion catches many
   auxiliary surfaces, but the explicit requirement to collapse multiple
   surfaces representing one task window—without collapsing two genuine
   documents of one application—has no implementation or two-surface fixture.
8. GREEN MEMBER RECTS ARE SNAPSHOTTED ONLY AT PICK BEGIN. If a selected window
   moves during the session, its required green outline remains at the old
   bounds. Green outlines must track resolved current member bounds at the
   bounded sampling rate, without animation.
Red/blue hue tuning remains an eye-check matter. The primary-monitor tie nuance
is low risk and is not added to 016R.

IMMEDIATE ADDENDUM FOR WINTER

  narrowly and add discriminating fixtures/tests.
- Replace CDP/synthetic evidence for the acceptance rows with real OS cursor,

  assertion record cursor point, foreground HWND, target HWND/bounds/show

  OS click is issued after begin invocation without waiting for a hover sample.

- Continue using production successful-null responses as the blank oracle.
  Timeout, helper failure and missing samples are never null.

ADDENDUM FOR ROKETPUNCHA

[2026-08-10 Asia/Saigon] BRAIN - WINTER LIVE DPI CONTRADICTION RULING
======================================================================

OBSERVED CONTRADICTION
On a non-primary/scaled display, the production sampler reports successful
`hover=null` at a stationary cursor point, but a physical click at that same
apparent point selects Settings/another window. This is NOT evidence that no
blank point exists, and it must not be converted into an environmental-pass
row. It is evidence that the sampler/helper and native click may be using
different coordinate spaces.

ONE DECISIVE MEASUREMENT — DO THIS BEFORE MORE THEORY
At one stationary point on the 100% primary and one on a scaled display, record
in the same instant:
- native Win32 `GetCursorPos` physical coordinates;
- Electron `screen.getCursorScreenPoint()` coordinates;
- containing display bounds and `scaleFactor`;
- the exact x/y sent to helper `hover` and the HWND/bounds it resolves;
- the overlay's calculated screen click x/y.
Do not infer any of these values. Compare the observed ratios/offsets to the
display scale factor and negative monitor origin.

DECISION AFTER MEASUREMENT
- If the spaces differ, establish one canonical boundary and convert exactly
  once. Electron display/BrowserWindow coordinates are DIP; the Win32 helper
  and physical input must receive the coordinate space their APIs actually
  observe. Use Electron's supported screen conversion where applicable; do
  not hand-roll a scale multiplication that breaks negative/mixed-DPI origins.
  Add a mixed-DPI/negative-origin regression around the conversion boundary.
- Once a blank oracle is established, the native input primitive must issue
  button down/up at the CURRENT cursor location without repositioning it from
  a coordinate expressed in another space.
- A successful production-null immediately followed by a picked window remains
  a FAIL until the coordinate contradiction is resolved. The approved fallback
  applies only when no successful-null point exists—not when null and click
  disagree.

OTHER CURRENT HARNESS DECISIONS
- Comparing show-state objects by JSON key order is invalid; compare fields.
- After a successful pick destroys the focused overlay, Windows may promote the
  underlying topmost window. Prove overlay focus immediately BEFORE input and
  prove target bounds/show/membership are unchanged AFTER; do not require a
  destroyed overlay to remain foreground.
- Remove the temporary `DEBUG-HOVER` output before checkpoint.

WINTER: time-box this measurement to 20 minutes. If values do not identify a
single conversion boundary, append the five observed values and STOP for BRAIN
instead of generating another system-level hypothesis tree.
